OUR REVISED R THEORY
VOLUME III 


Publication Architecture
Volume III is organized by the dependency grammar RAPIDITY → BRIDGE → GATE → OBSTRUCTION. A rapidity is an exact additive or logarithmic coordinate on relative state data; it parameterizes a relation but supplies no traversal law. A bridge is a lawful map carrying already-earned structure into a new representation, geometric, or effective description without promoting the target physics. A gate is an explicit condition that must be satisfied before a downstream physical claim is admitted. An obstruction is a theorem, no-go result, or missing resource that shows why the present candidate class cannot cross that gate. These roles are distinct: a bridge may expose a gate, and a failed bridge sharpens the obstruction.


By this grammar, Book 14 develops relational mass and motion rapidities, their elliptic continuation, and the spinor/symmetric-square bridges, then stops at the correlation gate exposed by the one-body no-quadrupole theorem. Book 15 uses obstruction complementarity to build the E8 internal/geometric, coframe, chirality, condensate, and Saw/Hodge bridges and reduces the remaining debts to explicit action, parameter, and vacuum gates. Book 16 takes the invariant relative-mismatch rapidity into the Hodge/coframe/BF-PCBF bridge stack and terminates at the nonzero-rank, unsoldered-branch, healthy-spectrum, full-E8 flux-transfer, and absolute-mirror gates. Physical cosmological evolution is downstream of those gates, not an input to them.


Governing Dependency Rule
Nothing is promoted by resemblance or notation. A rapidity is not a clock; a bridge is not a derivation of the target dynamics; a gate is not crossed because a representation exists; and an obstruction is not discharged unless the proposed extension supplies the exact missing resource while preserving prior theorems. Obstruction complementarity remains the search rule: two obstructions are jointly reduced only when one independently motivated extension crosses both corresponding gates with fewer premises than separate repairs. If a bridge fails, the failure is retained as an obstruction and the gate remains open.

BOOK 14 — TWO-FERMION MASS GEOMETRY AND THE SPINOR BRIDGE
STATUS: CANONICAL CONSOLIDATION; EXACT RELATIONAL AND BOUND-COORDINATE THEOREMS; CONDITIONAL DIRAC WITNESS; NO EMPIRICAL PROMOTION


14.0 Purpose, source retirement, and dependency boundary


Book 14 begins Volume III by consolidating the part of historical T5 that survives the revised dependency audit. Historical T5 combined two logically different projects: an independently declared three-state/color carrier and a two-fermion mass/binding geometry. The first is no longer needed as a premise because Books 5–6 and Book 11 now earn the complex 2+3 carrier and its particle-representation architecture independently. The second remains valuable and is re-entered here in canonical form.


Accordingly, Book 14 inherits only:


• the certified reciprocal and half-angle calculus of Books 0–7;
• the relativistic and spinorial projection discipline of Books 9–10;
• ordinary special-relativistic two-body kinematics as an explicit physics import;
• ordinary bound-state and Dirac–Coulomb physics only where named;
• the empirical-promotion firewall of the revised theory.


Book 14 does not inherit historical T5’s independent C³ color declaration, tetrahedral color interpretation, confinement analogies, or harmonic mass hypotheses. Those remain archival provenance rather than active premises.


Book 14 therefore supplies the first rapidity layer of Volume III. Its task is to separate exact relative coordinates from physical dynamics, carry only those coordinates that survive independent checks through the Dirac and symmetric-square bridges, and identify the first downstream gate.


The governing question is: which two-body rapidities and projective coordinates are exact rewritings of supplied data, which bridges preserve them into the spinor and spin-1 operator descriptions, and what obstruction prevents that two-channel structure from being promoted directly into three-family dynamics?


14.I Relational mass rapidity


Let m₁,m₂>0 be two supplied masses. Define the logarithmic mass rapidity


λ_m = ln(m₁/m₂)


and its Cayley half-coordinate


q_m = tanh(λ_m/2).


Theorem 14.I.T1 — Relational Mass Coordinate


The mass coordinate is exactly


q_m = (m₁-m₂)/(m₁+m₂),


with inverse


m₁/m₂ = (1+q_m)/(1-q_m).


Thus q_m is a bounded representation of a supplied positive mass ratio. It does not generate either mass.


Corollary 14.I.C1 — Hyperbolic identities


cosh λ_m = (1+q_m²)/(1-q_m²),


sinh λ_m = 2q_m/(1-q_m²).


Corollary 14.I.C2 — Normalized-square / downstream qSaw compatibility
For any complementary positive pair u+d=1, define projective odds Ω=u/d, rapidity η=ln Ω, and imbalance q=u-d=tanh(η/2). Normalize the componentwise square by u_2=u²/(u²+d²), d_2=d²/(u²+d²). Then Ω_2=Ω², η_2=2η, and q_2=u_2-d_2=2q/(1+q²)=tanh η. The coherence companion is c_2=2ud/(u²+d²)=(1-q²)/(1+q²), with q_2²+c_2²=1. Book 15 identifies this exact normalized-square operation with qSaw when u,d are the complementary shares derived from the primitive Saw pair. No additional absolute-value prescription is introduced.


The mass-asymmetry coordinate therefore belongs to the same Cayley/hyperbolic vocabulary already certified elsewhere in the theory, but its physical input is the supplied pair of masses.


14.II Two-body invariant and independence of motion


Physics Import 14.II.P1 — Standard two-particle relativity


For future-directed on-shell four-momenta p₁,p₂ with masses m₁,m₂, define relative rapidity η by


p₁·p₂ = m₁m₂ cosh η.


Then the invariant pair mass is


s = (p₁+p₂)²
  = m₁²+m₂²+2m₁m₂ cosh η.


Using λ_m,


s/(2m₁m₂) = cosh λ_m + cosh η.


Define the independent motion coordinate


q_v = tanh(η/2).


Theorem 14.II.T1 — Rational Pair-Invariant Form


The standard invariant becomes


s/(4m₁m₂)
=
(1-q_m² q_v²)/[(1-q_m²)(1-q_v²)].


This is an exact coordinate rewrite of standard two-body relativity.


Negative Theorem 14.II.N1 — Mass Asymmetry Does Not Determine Relative Motion


The supplied mass ratio fixes q_m but leaves η, and therefore q_v, free. Hence


q_m ↛ q_v.


No mass-only rule of Book 14 determines relative velocity, center-of-mass energy, or interaction dynamics.


14.III Elliptic continuation below threshold


Declared Mathematical Continuation 14.III.E1


Continue the relative rapidity by


η = iθ,


with θ real on a selected elliptic chart, and define


u = tan(θ/2).


Then


cosh η = cos θ = (1-u²)/(1+u²).


The analytic continuation is mathematics. Interpreting the continued invariant as a physical bound state requires independent dynamics that actually produce a pole or eigenstate below threshold.


Theorem 14.III.T1 — Continued Two-Fermion Invariant


Writing M²=s on the continued branch,


M²/(4m₁m₂)
=
(1+q_m²u²)/[(1-q_m²)(1+u²)].


Theorem 14.III.T2 — Exact Bound-Mass Coordinate


Solving for u gives


u²
=
[(m₁+m₂)²-M²]
/
[M²-(m₁-m₂)²].


For


|m₁-m₂|<M<m₁+m₂,


u is real and positive.


The inverse relation is


M²
=
(m₁+m₂)²
(1+q_m²u²)/(1+u²).


This equation is the central exact mass geometry of Book 14. It is invertible: supplied m₁,m₂,M determine u, and supplied m₁,m₂,u determine M. By itself it predicts neither the existence nor the value of a physical bound-state mass.


14.IV Binding energy and reduced-mass limit


Let


M = m₁+m₂-B,


where B is a supplied positive binding energy. Then


u²
=
B[2(m₁+m₂)-B]
/
[(2m₁-B)(2m₂-B)].


Let


μ = m₁m₂/(m₁+m₂)


be the reduced mass.


Theorem 14.IV.T1 — Weak-Binding Expansion


For B small compared with both constituent masses,


u² = B/(2μ)+O(B²),


or equivalently


B = 2μu²+O(u⁴).


After importing ordinary Coulomb binding,


B_n ≈ μ(Zα)²/(2n²),


one obtains the conditional weak-coupling check


u ≈ Zα/(2n).


The Coulomb law is imported; the half-angle scaling is its coordinate image.


Negative Theorem 14.IV.N1 — Binding Is Independent Data


The mass-asymmetry coordinate q_m does not determine u. Equal or nearly equal constituent masses can support very different binding coordinates, and strongly unequal masses can support weak binding. Any proposed universal law u=f(q_m) requires new dynamics not present here.


14.V Heavy-source convergence and the Dirac bridge


Take the heavy-source limit


m₁→∞,


M=m₁+E,


where E is the positive total energy of the light fermion of mass m₂.


Theorem 14.V.T1 — Heavy-Source Half-Angle Limit


The exact bound coordinate satisfies


u² → (m₂-E)/(m₂+E).


Define a bound-energy angle χ_E by


cos χ_E = E/m₂.


Then


u = tan(χ_E/2)


on the positive branch.


Physics Import 14.V.P1 — Circular radial Dirac–Coulomb state


For the standard point-Coulomb Dirac problem on the circular n_r=0, κ<0 branch, the radial components have constant projective ratio satisfying


|G/F|² = (m₂-E)/(m₂+E).


Therefore:


Theorem 14.V.T2 — Independent Bound-Coordinate Convergence


On the common positive heavy-source circular Dirac–Coulomb branch,


u = |G/F| = sxp(χ_E).


The equality is exact after the standard Dirac–Coulomb import. It is the strongest surviving physical witness from historical T5 because the two sides arise from independent constructions: one from the analytically continued two-body invariant, the other from the radial spinor solution.


Negative Theorem 14.V.N1 — Constant-Ratio Boundary


Generic radially excited Dirac states require a running Prüfer phase and an independent amplitude. The constant identification u=|G/F| therefore does not extend unchanged to a complete excited radial wavefunction.


14.VI Two-component operator form


Set


u = tan α,


and define the normalized real two-channel state


χ_u
=
(1,u)^T/sqrt(1+u²)
=
(cos α,sin α)^T.


Define the Hermitian doublet operator


K_m = diag(1,q_m²).


Theorem 14.VI.T1 — Bound-Mass Expectation Theorem


The exact bound-mass relation is


M²/(m₁+m₂)²
=
<χ_u|K_m|χ_u>.


Thus Book 14 admits an exact operator/state separation:


• q_m determines the diagonal mass-asymmetry operator K_m;
• u determines the state orientation χ_u;
• neither determines the other.


Corollary 14.VI.C1 — Double-angle readout


Because u=tan α,


M²/(m₁+m₂)²
=
(1+q_m²)/2
+
(1-q_m²)cos(2α)/2.


Differentiating,


d/dα [M²/(m₁+m₂)²]
=
-(1-q_m²)sin(2α).


The same cosine-imbalance and sine-balance pair certified in Book 7 therefore appears inside the exact two-body mass coordinate after the state angle is declared.


14.VII Square-root mass state and the Veronese embedding


For positive supplied masses define the normalized square-root mass doublet


χ_m
=
( sqrt(m₁), sqrt(m₂) )^T
/
sqrt(m₁+m₂).


Write


χ_m=(cos θ_m,sin θ_m)^T.


Theorem 14.VII.T1 — Mass–Half-Angle Identity


cos(2θ_m)=q_m,


sin(2θ_m)=2sqrt(m₁m₂)/(m₁+m₂)=sqrt(1-q_m²).


Equivalently,


cos(2θ_m)=tanh(λ_m/2),


sin(2θ_m)=sech(λ_m/2).


This gives an exact parameter-free bridge between the hyperbolic mass-ratio coordinate and a circular double-angle coordinate.


Definition 14.VII.D1 — Symmetric-square coherent-state lift


For any normalized doublet χ=(c,s)^T define


ν₂(χ)
=
(c², sqrt(2)cs, s²)^T.


Projectively,


[u:v] → [u²:sqrt(2)uv:v²]


is the degree-two Veronese map CP¹→CP².


Clarification 14.VII.CL1 — Conic Rank Firewall


The Veronese image is a conic inside CP². A pure symmetric square of one doublet does not generate all of CP² and does not by itself derive three physical generations. This firewall is inherited by Book 15.


For the square-root mass state,


ν₂(χ_m)
=
(
m₁/(m₁+m₂),
sqrt(2m₁m₂)/(m₁+m₂),
m₂/(m₁+m₂)
)^T.


This is an exact coherent-state embedding of supplied two-mass data into a three-component spin-1 module.


14.VIII Symmetric-square one-body lift


Let K be any Hermitian operator on C². Define its averaged one-body lift to Sym²(C²) by


K^(2)
=
1/2(K⊗I+I⊗K)|_Sym².


Theorem 14.VIII.T1 — Coherent-State Expectation Preservation


For every normalized χ∈C² and Φ=χ⊙χ∈Sym²(C²),


<Φ|K^(2)|Φ>
=
<χ|K|χ>.


For the mass operator K_m,


K_m^(2)
=
diag(1,(1+q_m²)/2,q_m²).


Let


J_z=diag(1,0,-1).


Then


K_m^(2)
=
(1+q_m²)I/2
+
(1-q_m²)J_z/2.


Therefore the exact Book 14 bound-state observable lifts into the spin-1 module without changing its value.


If Φ_u=ν₂(χ_u), then


<Φ_u|J_z|Φ_u>=cos(2α),


and with the standard spin-1


J_x
=
1/sqrt(2)
[0 1 0;
 1 0 1;
 0 1 0],


one has


<Φ_u|J_x|Φ_u>=sin(2α).


The double-angle carrier is therefore the expectation geometry of the symmetric-square coherent-state locus.


14.IX One-body lift and the correlation boundary


A general Hermitian two-state operator has four real components and may be written


K=k₀I+k_i σ_i/2.


Its symmetric-square one-body lift has the form


K^(2)=k₀I+(1/2)k_iJ_i.


Theorem 14.IX.T1 — One-Body Lift No-Quadrupole Theorem


The image of the natural Hermitian one-body lifting map


Herm(C²) → Herm(Sym²(C²))


is exactly the scalar-plus-dipole sector


1 ⊕ 3.


It does not contain the five-dimensional spin-2/quadrupole sector.


Since


Herm(Sym²(C²))
=
1 ⊕ 3 ⊕ 5,


the remaining five real Hermitian directions are genuine two-copy correlations rather than disguised one-body terms.


For example, with s_i=σ_i/2 and


J_i=s_i⊗I+I⊗s_i,


one has on the symmetric subspace


Q_xz
:=
{J_x,J_z}/2
=
s_x⊗s_z+s_z⊗s_x.


Thus the first mixed quadrupole is literally a correlation between the two underlying factors.


This theorem establishes the exact boundary between Book 14 and Book 15:


Book 14 exhausts the natural one-body lift of the two-channel mass geometry. Any later three-family flavor structure requiring the quadrupole 5 must introduce genuinely new correlation dynamics.


14.X Empirical control and failure discipline


The historical T5 control ladder is retained only for status discipline.


Positronium supplies the equal-mass pointlike limit q_m=0.


Muonium supplies a strongly asymmetric but pointlike-lepton comparison.


Hydrogen supplies an absolute ionization threshold and a controlled Coulombic coordinate.


The deuteron supplies a hadronic threshold with nearly equal constituent masses and a substantially different binding coordinate.


These systems demonstrate that the coordinate is broadly applicable as bookkeeping while q_m and u remain independent.


Negative-result ledger:


• a transition frequency alone does not determine an absolute u without a threshold or spectral anchor;
• u is not universally α/2;
• q_m does not determine u;
• deuteron binding is not derived by the coordinate;
• light-quark mass substitution is not a clean test because confined quark masses are scheme-dependent parameters;
• the historical 81/32 neutron–proton/electron relation is not exact;
• harmonic or musical proximity is not a mass law;
• a measured mass or threshold used to construct u is not an independent prediction of u;
• no present relation supplies a new bound-state spectrum or interaction law.


The numerical proximity of any post-data rational candidate remains a historical clue only unless a future action fixes it before the observable is used.

BOOK 15 — SAW INTERCHANGES, DOUBLE-ANGLE DECOMPOSITION, AND THE BRIDGE TO E8

15.0 Purpose and inherited boundary
Book 15 is the bridge book of Volume III. It begins with the exact rapidity/reciprocity output of Book 14 and asks how that geometry is realized by the native R operators, their complementary interchange, the aligned primitive differences, the double-angle decomposition, and the signed Saw transfer. Only after those structures are closed may the book cross to the separately established and explicitly admitted E8(-24) representation host used by the Book-16 gateway analysis.
Inherited from Book 14 without re-proof:
• positive-ratio rapidity η=ln R and Cayley coordinate q=tanh(η/2);
• projective odds Ω=λ/(1-λ), log-odds η=ln Ω, and q=2λ-1;
• reciprocal reflection λ↔1-λ and η↔-η;
• the square-root circular pair (cos2θ,sin2θ)=(q,sqrt(1-q²));
• the positive-chart UNA access pair urx=1/λ and uxp=1/(1-λ);
• the displaced reciprocal pair and square-root hyperbola;
• the symmetric-square coherent-state geometry and one-body/correlation boundary.
Canonical notation lock:
srx(x)=|csc x|+cot x,
sxp(x)=1/(|csc x|+cot x),
cxp(x)=|sec x|+tan x,
crx(x)=1/(|sec x|+tan x),
urx=srx-crx,
uxp=cxp-sxp.
The sign convention uxp=cxp-sxp is authoritative throughout this book.
15.I Canonical UNA Complementary-Swap Closure
Work away from the scalar seams where the displayed trigonometric primitives are finite. Let the complementary reflection be
C(x)=π/2-x.
The reciprocal identities give
sxp=|csc x|-cot x,
crx=|sec x|-tan x.
Theorem 15.I.T1 — Primitive Complementary Interchange
Under C,
srx(Cx)=cxp(x),
cxp(Cx)=srx(x),
sxp(Cx)=crx(x),
crx(Cx)=sxp(x).
Therefore
urx(Cx)=uxp(x),
uxp(Cx)=urx(x).
Proof. Substitute sin(π/2-x)=cos x and cos(π/2-x)=sin x into the absolute reciprocal definitions. The UNA exchange follows immediately from the canonical differences. ∎
Definition 15.I.D1 — Complex UNA package
Define
Ψ_U(x)=urx(x)+i uxp(x).
Then complementary reflection gives
Ψ_U(Cx)=uxp+i urx=i conjugate(Ψ_U(x)).
Corollary 15.I.C1 — Involutive complex closure
Applying C twice returns the original package. The complementary map is therefore an involution on the scalar state geometry, not a first-order propagation equation.
Module M15-A output contract
Downstream modules may use the exact primitive permutation, the swap urx↔uxp, and the complex closure Ψ_U(Cx)=i conjugate(Ψ_U(x)). Complementation is an exact state-space involution.
M15-A firewall
The complementary swap does not generate physical time, transport, a Hamiltonian, chirality, Hodge duality, or product duality. A discrete involution is not by itself a dynamical evolution law.
15.II Aligned Primitive-Difference Hyperbola
Definition 15.II.D1 — Aligned primitive differences
Define
A(x)=srx-sxp,
B(x)=cxp-crx.
Using the reciprocal forms,
A=2cot x,
B=2tan x.
Theorem 15.II.T1 — Primitive Product Closure
On the common finite domain,
A B=4.
Thus A and B are an exact reciprocal hyperbolic pair.
Definition 15.II.D2 — Even and odd aligned combinations
To avoid collision with later operator gradings, define
D_2:=A+B,
K_2:=A-B.
Because
urx+uxp
=(srx-crx)+(cxp-sxp)
=(srx-sxp)+(cxp-crx),
one has
D_2=urx+uxp.
Theorem 15.II.T2 — UNA Hyperbola
The aligned combinations satisfy
D_2²-K_2²=4AB=16.
Under complementary reflection C,
A↔B,
D_2→D_2,
K_2→-K_2.
Hence D_2 is the complement-even coordinate and K_2 the complement-odd coordinate of the same exact hyperbola.
Module M15-B1 output contract
Downstream modules may inherit A=2cot x, B=2tan x, AB=4, D_2=urx+uxp, K_2=A-B, and D_2²-K_2²=16, with C acting as the odd reflection K_2→-K_2.
M15-B1 firewall
The hyperbola is algebraic state geometry. Its even/odd coordinates do not by themselves define energy, momentum, spacetime signature, or a physical boost generator.
15.III Canonical Double-Angle Decomposition and Phasor
Theorem 15.III.T1 — Exact Double-Angle Reconstruction
The aligned variables reduce exactly to
D_2=2(cot x+tan x)=4/sin(2x),
K_2=2(cot x-tan x)=4cot(2x).
Therefore
sin(2x)=4/D_2,
cos(2x)=K_2/D_2.
Theorem 15.III.T2 — Canonical Double-Angle Phasor
Define
Z_2(x):=(K_2+4i)/D_2.
Then
Z_2=cos(2x)+i sin(2x)=e^{2ix}.
Thus the continuous double-angle phase is reconstructed exactly from the aligned primitive-difference hyperbola.
Definition 15.III.D1 — Balance and imbalance readouts
Define
H_R:=1/D_2=sin(2x)/4,
V_R:=K_2/(2D_2)=cos(2x)/2.
Then
H_R'=V_R,
V_R'=-4H_R,
Z_2'=2i Z_2.
Corollary 15.III.C1 — Complement action
Because C leaves sin(2x) fixed and reverses cos(2x),
H_R(Cx)=H_R(x),
V_R(Cx)=-V_R(x),
Z_2(Cx)=-conjugate(Z_2(x))=e^{i(π-2x)}.
Module M15-B2 output contract
The native R primitives reconstruct the exact continuous double-angle circle through the hyperbolic pair (D_2,K_2). Downstream modules may use H_R=sin2x/4, V_R=cos2x/2, and Z_2=e^{2ix}, together with their complement parity.
M15-B2 firewall
The phasor Z_2 is an exact mathematical phase coordinate. It is not automatically a quantum phase, gauge phase, physical clock, Hodge quarter phase, or E8 grading phase. Those identifications require separate bridges.
15.IV Signed Saw Complement and Transfer
This module derives the signed Saw transfer directly from the primitive absolute-valued core operators and aligns it with the double-angle reconstruction above. The absolute values belong only to srx and cxp; no new downstream absolute-value operation is introduced.
Away from scalar seams define the core Saw pair
sawdown:=srx/(cxp·srx-1),
sawup:=cxp/(cxp·srx-1).
Their common chart sign is
ε:=sawup+sawdown=FlatWave=sgn(sin2x).
On the corresponding bounded complementary chart let
0≤λ≤1,
ε=FlatWave=sgn(sin2x)
away from the seams. The core formulas reduce exactly to the signed complementary pair
sawup=ελ,
sawdown=ε(1-λ),
with projective odds
Ω=λ/(1-λ).
Define the intrinsic Saw product magnitude
H_Saw:=sawup·sawdown=λ(1-λ),
while the signed double-angle harmonic carrier from 15.III is
H_R=sin(2x)/4=ε H_Saw.
Thus the Saw product supplies the unsigned magnitude already folded by the core absolute-valued operators, while ε restores the signed harmonic carrier:
H_R=1/D_2=ε H_Saw.
Theorem 15.IV.T1 — Signed Saw Complement
Under complementary transfer λ↔1-λ,
sawup↔sawdown,
Ω↔Ω^{-1},
ln Ω↔-ln Ω,
while ε and H_Saw remain unchanged on the complementary scalar pair x↔π/2-x; consequently H_R=εH_Saw is unchanged there as well.
The bounded imbalance
q=2λ-1
is odd under the complement, while the intrinsic Saw magnitude H_Saw is even.
Corollary 15.IV.C1 — Square-root transfer angle
Let θ_λ be the Book-14 square-root angle, so λ=cos²θ_λ. Define the equivalent full transfer angle θ:=π-2θ_λ. Then
λ=sin²(θ/2),
1-λ=cos²(θ/2),
q=-cos θ,
2sqrt(H_Saw)=sin θ,
Ω=tan²(θ/2).
This is the same Book-14 circle written with the complementary full-angle orientation θ=π-2θ_λ. The signed Saw pair is therefore a signed squared-component lift of the same bounded transfer state; no new continuous degree of freedom has been introduced.
Definition 15.IV.D1 — Canonical qSaw normalized square
Factor the common Saw sign rather than introducing any new absolute value:
u:=sawup/ε=λ,
d:=sawdown/ε=1-λ.
Then u+d=1, u,d≥0, H_Saw=ud, and the local reciprocal imbalance is
q:=u-d=2λ-1.
The qSaw step is exactly one normalized componentwise square,
(u,d) → (u_q,d_q):=(u²,d²)/(u²+d²).
Therefore
qSaw(q):=u_q-d_q=2q/(1+q²),
c_q:=2ud/(u²+d²)=(1-q²)/(1+q²),
with qSaw(q)²+c_q²=1.
If Ω=u/d and η=ln Ω, then Ω→Ω² and η→2η. Since q=tanh(η/2),
qSaw(q)=tanh η.
Equivalently, writing w=η/2 gives qSaw:tanh w→tanh(2w). Thus qSaw is the normalized degree-doubling/double-rapidity map already implicit in the core Saw pair, not an independent phenomenological transfer.
Inverse-qSaw is the corresponding oriented square-root descent: halve the rapidity, or take the inherited-branch square roots of the quadratic shares and renormalize. The qSaw step is applied once at the degree-doubling transition.
Orientation firewall. The common sheet sign ε is factored out before normalized squaring, so qSaw intensity data cannot by itself recover ε. Saw complementation, qSaw degree doubling, and the later product-duality/Hodge orientation are distinct operations.
Module M15-C output contract
Downstream modules may use the exact distinction between the bounded complement λ↔1-λ, the inherited common Saw sign ε, the core channels sawup/sawdown, the intrinsic product magnitude H_Saw, and the one-step qSaw normalized square. The complement exchanges the Saw channels but does not erase ε; qSaw factors ε out and therefore cannot reconstruct it from quadratic intensity alone.
M15-C firewall
The Saw complement is not product duality, Hodge duality, or a quarter-turn on a normal-amplitude plane. The sign ε is not the later Hodge/bridge orientation sign s_Ω. Those distinctions are the subject of later modules.
15.V Quarter-Phase Saw–Hodge Lift and Complementary Sign Visibility
Status: exact two-state/Hermitian algebra plus an explicitly conditional Hodge/bridge interpretation. The independent phase introduced here is not generated by the one-scalar Saw calculus itself.
Definition 15.V.D1 — Square-root phase lift
For 0≤λ≤1 define
ψ(λ,φ)=(sqrt λ, e^{iφ}sqrt(1-λ))^T.
Its pure-state coherence obeys
|ρ_12|²=λ(1-λ)=H_Saw.
Let
s_Ω∈{+1,-1}
denote the orientation of a chosen quarter-phase branch. This sign is independent of the inherited Saw/quadrant sign ε. Set
φ=s_Ω π/2,
ψ_{s_Ω}(λ)=(sqrt λ, i s_Ω sqrt(1-λ))^T.
Theorem 15.V.T1 — Saw–Hodge Meridian Theorem
The Hermitian projector is
ρ_{s_Ω}(λ)=ψ_{s_Ω}ψ_{s_Ω}†
=1/2[I+(2λ-1)Z+2s_Ω sqrt(H_Saw)Y].
Hence
<X>=0,
<Y>=2s_Ω sqrt(H_Saw),
<Z>=2λ-1.
The quarter-phase lift therefore lies exactly on a Y–Z meridian of the two-state sphere and saturates the pure-state coherence ceiling.
Corollary 15.V.C1 — Balance states and reflection
At λ=1/2,
ρ_{s_Ω}=1/2(I+s_ΩY),
so the two balanced quarter-phase states are the ±Y eigenstates.
Define the reflection
R_Y:I→I, X→-X, Y→Y, Z→-Z.
Then
R_Y:ρ_{s_Ω}(λ)→ρ_{s_Ω}(1-λ).
The reflection exchanges complementary transfer weights but preserves the Y axis and therefore does not choose +s_Ω over -s_Ω.
Definition 15.V.D2 — Projective phase coordinate
To avoid collision with the log-odds/mismatch rapidity ζ used elsewhere in Volume III, denote the projective component ratio by z_proj. Then
z_proj:=sqrt λ/[i s_Ω sqrt(1-λ)]
=-i s_Ω sqrt Ω,
so
|z_proj|²=Ω.
The projective odds supply the radius while s_Ω supplies the sign of the quarter-phase argument.
Definition 15.V.D3 — Signed quadratic lift
Choose a unit-modulus factor σ_ε satisfying
σ_ε²=ε,
and define
ξ_{ε,s_Ω}=σ_ε ψ_{s_Ω}.
Theorem 15.V.T2 — Complementary Sign-Visibility Theorem
The Hermitian projector satisfies
ξ_{ε,s_Ω}ξ_{ε,s_Ω}†=ρ_{s_Ω},
so it is independent of ε but records s_Ω through the sign of the Y coherence.
The componentwise symmetric square is
Q_C(ξ_{ε,s_Ω})
=(ξ_1²,ξ_2²)^T
=(ελ,-ε(1-λ))^T.
Thus the symmetric square records ε but is independent of s_Ω because s_Ω²=1.
Away from the endpoints, the Hermitian and symmetric quadratic constructions jointly recover the two independent binary data (ε,s_Ω), while neither construction alone contains both.
Corollary 15.V.C2 — Signed-Saw residue
The signed Saw pair is
(sawup,sawdown)=(ελ, ε(1-λ)),
whereas the component square of the quarter-phase lift is
(ελ,-ε(1-λ)).
The minus sign is not an error to be repaired. It is exactly the algebraic residue of the π/2 relative phase. Therefore the Hodge quarter-phase lift is not a literal simultaneous componentwise square root of both signed Saw channels.
Theorem 15.V.T3 — Protected-Traversal Obstruction
Using the full transfer angle θ of 15.IV, the Bloch vector is
r_{s_Ω}(θ)=(0,s_Ω sinθ,-cosθ).
If θ is promoted to a parameter of closed unitary evolution, its tangent is generated by an X-type operator:
dρ/dθ=-i[(s_Ω/2)X,ρ].
An R_Y-even closed Hamiltonian restricted to the I/Y operator algebra therefore cannot by itself generate nontrivial traversal along the meridian. Traversal requires an X-odd driver, symmetry breaking, an enlarged dynamical sector, or a non-closed/nonunitary mechanism.
Module M15-D output contract
Downstream modules may use two distinct binary data: ε for inherited signed Saw/quadrant orientation and s_Ω for quarter-phase/Hodge-bridge orientation. The quarter-phase lift gives an exact Y–Z meridian, the Hermitian and symmetric quadratic constructions have complementary sign visibility, and the structural minus sign under componentwise squaring is mandatory.
M15-D firewall
The exact two-state algebra does not select an absolute sign s_Ω, derive a physical Hodge star, supply a Hamiltonian, identify θ with time, or prove an E8 embedding. The word “Hodge” remains a conditional bridge interpretation until the later geometric gateway supplies the relevant operator matching.
15.VI Saw Complement, Native Quarter-Turn, and Product-Duality Bridge
Status: exact native transfer-circle and symmetric-square theorems; exact distinction between Saw complement and quarter-turn complex structure; identification with a later physical E8 normal-plane product-duality action belongs to the Book-16 gateway.
Definition 15.VI.D1 — Native normalized transfer doublet
On an open scalar quadrant inherit the certified Book-7 transfer coordinates. Let α=sgn(sin x) and β=sgn(cos x), constant on that quadrant, and define p(x)=α cos x-β sin x and q(x)=α sin x+β cos x. Then these real functions satisfy
p²+q²=2,
p'=-q,
q'=p,
and let the bounded Saw share be
λ=(1-p)/2.
Define
t(x)=(1/sqrt2)(q,p)^T.
Then
t^Tt=1.
Definition 15.VI.D2 — Native quarter-turn generator
Define
J=[[0,1],[-1,0]].
Then
J²=-I,
and the transfer doublet obeys
t'=Jt.
Theorem 15.VI.T1 — Saw Complement and Quarter-Turn Are Distinct
The Saw complement
C_S: λ→1-λ
is a reflected transfer-state operation of order two. The native quarter-turn
C_J:t→Jt
is an oriented circle operation satisfying
C_J²:t→-t,
C_J⁴=identity.
Therefore Saw complement and the quarter-turn generated by J are not the same transformation. They must not be identified even when both interchange or reverse derived two-channel data.
Proof. The Saw complement squares to the identity on λ. The matrix J satisfies J²=-I, so one application is a quarter rotation, two applications reverse the transfer vector, and four return it. The operations therefore have different order and act on different levels of the construction. ∎
Definition 15.VI.D3 — Traceless symmetric-square readout
Define
Q_t:=tt^T-(1/2)I.
Using the native transfer identities, Q_t has the exact double-angle form
Q_t=(ε/2)[sin(2x) Z + cos(2x) X],
where
Z=[[1,0],[0,-1]],
X=[[0,1],[1,0]],
and ε is the inherited signed-quadrant datum.
Theorem 15.VI.T2 — Symmetric-Square Double-Angle Lift
The primitive transfer state evolves with quarter-turn generator J, while its traceless symmetric-square readout evolves at doubled angular rate:
Q_t'=[J,Q_t],
Q_t''+4Q_t=0.
Moreover
Q_t²=(1/4)I.
Thus the symmetric-square lift rotates a fixed-norm traceless quadratic operator rather than changing its eigenvalue magnitude.
Corollary 15.VI.C1 — Quarter-Turn Oddness of the Quadratic Readout
Under the native quarter-turn
t→Jt,
one has
Q_t→JQ_tJ^T=-Q_t.
Hence both traceless symmetric channels X and Z are odd under the quarter-turn action.
Corollary 15.VI.C2 — Relation to the earlier double-angle phasor
The harmonic pair carried by Q_t is exactly the same pair reconstructed in 15.III from the aligned primitive hyperbola:
(sin2x,cos2x).
Thus Book 15 now has two exact routes to the same double-angle carrier:
primitive differences (A,B)
→ hyperbola (D_2,K_2)
→ phasor Z_2=e^{2ix},
and
normalized transfer doublet t
→ traceless symmetric square Q_t
→ harmonic pair (sin2x,cos2x).
The agreement is a structural consistency theorem, not a second degree of freedom.
Bridge Statement 15.VI.B1 — Product-Duality Matching Condition
A later physical two-normal amplitude quotient may realize product duality by a quarter-turn generator with the same algebra
J²=-I.
When the Book-16 E8 gateway identifies its physical normal-plane product-duality generator with the same J, the native Projection transfer circle supplies the exact pre-E8 transfer geometry for that action.
This bridge statement does not itself establish the E8 realization; that identification is a downstream representation/gateway theorem.
Module M15-E output contract
Downstream modules may inherit: Saw complement is an order-two reflection; the native transfer state is generated by an order-four quarter-turn J; its traceless symmetric square is product-duality-style odd and carries the exact double-angle harmonic pair; and the primitive-difference and symmetric-square routes reconstruct the same harmonic carrier.
M15-E firewall
Do not identify Saw complement with product duality. Do not infer that an abstract/native J is automatically the physical Hodge star, an O(1,1) real symmetry, or an E8(-24) automorphism. Book 16 must separately establish any physical normal-plane realization and its real-structure status.
15.VII Dual-Mode Operator Bigrading and Bridge Polarization
Status: exact finite-dimensional operator theorem; exact minimal matrix and bridge-polarization witnesses; E8 realization and action-level bridge purity remain downstream obligations.
Theorem 15.VII.T1 — Dual-Mode Operator Bigrading
Let V be a real finite-dimensional carrier equipped with two commuting complex structures I_ext and J_int satisfying
I_ext²=J_int²=-I,
[I_ext,J_int]=0.
Define involutions on End(V) by
σ_ext(A)=-I_ext A I_ext,
σ_int(A)=-J_int A J_int.
Then σ_ext and σ_int commute and End(V) decomposes into four simultaneous eigenspaces
End(V)=E_(++)⊕E_(+-)⊕E_(-+)⊕E_(--),
where
E_(εη)={A:σ_ext(A)=εA, σ_int(A)=ηA},
ε,η∈{+1,-1}.
Equivalently,
I_ext A=ε A I_ext,
J_int A=η A J_int.
The parity labels multiply under products and commutators:
E_(εη)E_(ε'η')⊂E_(εε',ηη'),
[E_(εη),E_(ε'η')]⊂E_(εε',ηη').
Proof. Each σ squares to the identity because its defining complex structure squares to -I. Their commutativity follows from [I_ext,J_int]=0. Simultaneous diagonalization under the two commuting involutions gives the decomposition, and the multiplication rules follow directly from the defining parity relations. ∎
Corollary 15.VII.C1 — Product-Parity Coarsening
Define
K:=I_ext J_int.
Then
K²=I.
For A∈E_(εη),
KA=(εη)AK.
Hence
K-even=E_(++)⊕E_(--),
K-odd=E_(+-)⊕E_(-+).
The single K parity remembers only the product εη. The dual-mode bigrading retains which of the two commuting complex-structure parities changed.
Corollary 15.VII.C2 — Relative complex-structure orientation
On a K eigenstate with eigenvalue k=±1,
J_int=-k I_ext.
Thus the two complex structures are equal or opposite up to sign on the two K sectors. A K-odd bridge transfers between relative-orientation sectors, while E_(+-) and E_(-+) distinguish which parity flips.
15.VII.1 Minimal real witness
Let
j=[[0,-1],[1,0]],
I_ext=j⊗I_2,
J_int=I_2⊗j.
Then I_ext²=J_int²=-I_4 and [I_ext,J_int]=0. The product K has two -1 and two +1 eigenvalues. Direct linear algebra gives
dim E_(++)=dim E_(+-)=dim E_(-+)=dim E_(--)=4
inside Mat(4,R), while the coarse K-even and K-odd spaces each have dimension eight.
This is an exact witness that the Z2×Z2 refinement can be nontrivial. It does not determine the dimensions of any later E8(-24) realization.
15.VII.2 Orthogonal bridge-polarization witness
Use a minimal complex two-state witness with
K=σ_z,
H=σ_x,
I_ext=iI,
J_int=-iK,
so I_ext J_int=K. Define the orthogonal bridge polarization
B:=I_ext K H=-σ_y.
Then
B†=B,
B²=I,
{K,B}=0,
{H,B}=0.
Under the J_int rotation,
H(θ)=exp[(θ/2)J_int]H exp[-(θ/2)J_int]
=cosθ H-sinθ B.
With the Hilbert-Schmidt product <A,C>=(1/2)Tr(A†C),
<H,H(θ)>=cosθ,
<B,H(θ)>=-sinθ.
Thus H and B are orthogonal cosine/sine bridge quadratures of the same phase rotation.
Corollary 15.VII.C3 — Generic bridge polarization does not select a quarter phase
For
B_δ=cosδ H+sinδ B,
one has
<B_δ,H(θ)>=cos(δ+θ).
A generic bridge polarization merely shifts the preferred phase. Exact quarter-phase preference is therefore not automatic; it requires a dynamical or representation constraint selecting the orthogonal B quadrature up to sign.
Conditional Theorem 15.VII.CT1 — Bridge-Purity Quarter-Phase Selection
Consider the candidate phase potential
V(θ)=-κ_x cosθ-s_Ω κ_y sinθ.
If the physical bridge is compelled by its later representation/action to occupy only the orthogonal B polarization, then
κ_x=0
exactly. For κ_y≠0 the stationary branch is
θ_*=+π/2 when s_Ωκ_y>0,
θ_*=-π/2 when s_Ωκ_y<0,
modulo 2π.
The result is conditional because the present operator theorem does not compel bridge purity and does not fix the sign of κ_y.
15.VII.3 Hyperbolic double-angle compatibility
Let an amplitude ratio be parameterized by
r=e^{-u},
r²=e^{-v},
so v=2u. With
λ=1/(1+r²),
m:=2λ-1,
one has
m=tanh(v/2)=tanh u,
tanh v=2m/(1+m²).
Thus the doubled rapidity coordinate and the bounded Cayley variable obey the standard hyperbolic double-angle law. This creates no second continuous degree of freedom: v=2u remains an exact relation. The genuinely new information in the dual-mode theorem is discrete operator parity, not an extra scalar coordinate.
Module M15-F output contract
Downstream modules may inherit the exact Z2×Z2 operator bigrading under two commuting complex structures, its K-parity coarsening, the existence of orthogonal H/B bridge quadratures, and the conditional statement that representation-enforced B-purity would remove the cosine coefficient and select a quarter-phase stationary branch.
M15-F firewall
The finite-dimensional witnesses do not prove that E8(-24), its adjoint, or the 30380 realizes the same joint-parity dimensions. Bridge purity, κ_x=0, and quarter-phase selection are not unconditional E8 theorems. The dual-mode bigrading is an operator bridge awaiting representation-level realization.
15.VIII Four-Dimensional Even-Exterior and Geometric-Amplitude Bridge
Status: exact finite-dimensional exterior-algebra and amplitude-coordinate identities; no physical soldering or cosmological equation is implied.
Definition 15.VIII.D1 — Relative amplitude coordinate
Let r>0 be a supplied positive amplitude ratio. Define
χ:=r²,
λ:=1/(1+r²),
1-λ=r²/(1+r²),
v:=ln[λ/(1-λ)]=-2ln r.
This is exactly the Book-14 positive-ratio/log-odds dictionary applied at amplitude level. Reciprocal amplitude exchange r↔r^{-1} gives
χ↔χ^{-1},
λ↔1-λ,
v↔-v.
Theorem 15.VIII.T1 — Four-Dimensional Even-Exterior Polynomial
Let M be any four-dimensional real or complex vector space. Then
dim Λ^0M=1,
dim Λ^2M=6,
dim Λ^4M=1.
Therefore the even-exterior generating polynomial is
Q_M(χ):=Σ_{j=0}^2 dim(Λ^{2j}M) χ^j
=1+6χ+χ².
The coefficient 6 is exactly C(4,2)=dim Λ²M.
Corollary 15.VIII.C1 — Reciprocal covariance
The polynomial obeys
χ² Q_M(χ^{-1})=Q_M(χ).
Equivalently,
Q_M(χ)/χ=χ+6+χ^{-1}
=2cosh v+6,
because χ=e^{-v}.
Thus the even-exterior polynomial is naturally organized by the same additive rapidity coordinate as the transfer geometry.
Definition 15.VIII.D2 — Mirror-weighted binomial family
For a real asymmetry parameter δ define
P_δ(r):=1+4δr+6r²+4δr³+r⁴.
Then
P_0(r)=1+6r²+r⁴=Q_M(r²),
P_{+1}(r)=(1+r)^4,
P_{-1}(r)=(1-r)^4.
Theorem 15.VIII.T2 — Parent Polynomial Identity
Let
A_δ(r):=δ(1+r²)+2r.
Then
P_δ(r)=A_δ(r)²+(1-δ²)(1+r²)².
For |δ|=1 the second term vanishes identically. At δ=0 the normalized ratio
Q_M(r²)/(4r²)
=[r²+r^{-2}+6]/4
=[cosh v+3]/2
has its unique positive minimum at
r=1 ⇔ χ=1 ⇔ λ=1/2 ⇔ v=0.
This is an algebraic balance theorem for the mirror-even polynomial family.
Module M15-G output contract
Downstream modules may inherit the exact amplitude dictionary r↔χ↔λ↔v, the four-dimensional even-exterior identity Q_M(χ)=1+6χ+χ², its reciprocal covariance, and the mirror-weighted binomial family P_δ(r) with the stated balance property.
M15-G firewall

Any E8 interpretation of the four-dimensional carrier or its two amplitude sectors belongs to Book 16.

15.IX.T3 — Canonical Round-Trip Normalization Theorem
THEOREM. Let a canonical UNA pair (urx,uxp) be carried into the reciprocal Saw chart by saw_r=1/urx and saw_x=1/uxp, and suppose a downstream realization returns to the same Saw chart with only a common scalar factor c. If the recovered endpoint is required to be the same canonical UNA pair in the same variables, quadrant signs, and reciprocal convention, then c=1.
Proof. Reciprocal recovery gives (urx,uxp)_out=(1/c)(urx,uxp)_in. Exact recovery of both canonical primitives therefore requires 1/c=1, hence c=1. ∎
COROLLARY — Relative-carrier closure. A lawful intermediate Saw/qSaw/symmetric-square or equivalent projective carrier cannot leave an independent common scalar normalization in the recovered relative carrier when the endpoint is exactly canonical.
SCOPE. This theorem does not fix a genuinely new action magnitude such as k_0=beta^2/(2 alpha), a later radial completion, a microscopic reduced matrix element, or the independent q_F=-4 UV matching datum.

16.0.A.1 — Rapidity Normalization and Thermodynamic-Lineage Firewall
Rapidity normalization firewall. The symbol ζ denotes the full additive/log-odds rapidity carried by the Book-16 mismatch spine. The symbol w denotes its hyperbolic half-coordinate,
ζ=2w.
Consequently,
Q=tanh w=tanh(ζ/2),
S=sech w=sech(ζ/2),
and one qSaw step gives
qSaw(Q)=tanh(2w)=tanh ζ.
The §16.I.59 single-hyperbola variables may therefore be written equivalently as
A_±=(μ/2)e^{±ζ},
Σ=μ cosh ζ,
Δ=μ sinh ζ,
Π_C=tanh ζ,
while the inverse-qSaw descent uses
Q=tanh(ζ/2).
The §16.I.60 transport remains written in half-rapidity normalization,
E(w)=exp[-(w/2)ad D]E_0,
A_D=-(1/2)dw;
in full-rapidity notation these are
E(ζ)=exp[-(ζ/4)ad D]E_0,
A_D=-(1/4)dζ.
This is a normalization identity, not a new rapidity and not a dynamical law.
Thermodynamic-lineage firewall. Book 13’s
v=ln[λ/(1−λ)]
is the thermodynamic/statistical natural coordinate and belongs to the same mathematical class of additive log-odds coordinates. Volume III preserves that rapidity architecture, but it does not identify the Book-13 λ with a Book-16 probability, field coordinate, or vacuum observable unless an independent bridge is proved. Thus v and ζ may share the log-odds form without being promoted to the same physical quantity. No later gate may use notation alone to turn this structural correspondence into dynamics.

16.I.49 — DIAGONAL Z BRIDGE AND COVARIANT 45-DEGREE STATIONARITY


Book 15's scalar doubled-phase phasor Z_2=e^(2ix) is one eigenvalue of the full real operator


calZ_2(x)=cos(2x)I+sin(2x)J=exp(2xJ),


J={{0,1},{-1,0}}, J^2=-I.


In the unitary eigenbasis U=(1/sqrt(2)){{1,1},{i,-i}},


U^(-1)JU=diag(i,-i)


and


U^(-1)calZ_2U=diag(e^(2ix),e^(-2ix))=diag(Z_2,Z_2^(-1)).


Thus the full operator carries the conjugate/reciprocal phase pair simultaneously. At x=pi/4,


Zdiag=diag(i,-i)=i sigma_3,


so Zdiag^2=-I and Zdiag^4=I. Complement x->pi/2-x exchanges the two branches with an overall minus sign and leaves the full diagonal operator fixed at x=pi/4.


Crucial correction: the full Z operator is not ordinarily stationary at 45 degrees. It obeys


d calZ_2/dx=2J calZ_2,


or in the diagonal basis


d Zdiag/dx=2i sigma_3 Zdiag.


At x=pi/4 the ordinary derivative is -2I.


Define the exact bridge connection


Gamma=2J


and covariant derivative


nabla_x=d/dx-Gamma.


Then


nabla_x calZ_2=0


identically. For the bounded bridge B_Z=H calZ_2 with H=sin(2x)/4,


nabla_x B_Z=H' calZ_2,


so at x=pi/4,


nabla_x B_Z=0


because H'(pi/4)=0. Hence the correct statement is covariant stationarity: the amplitude is stationary while the conjugate phase pair is parallel transported.


The holonomy is


Hol(x_1,x_0)=exp(2(x_1-x_0)J).


One 45-degree step gives J; four such steps give exp(2pi J)=I, reproducing both i^4/J^4 closure and the trivial full-cycle monodromy of the global D4/Mobius atlas.


This closes the phase-transport problem on the two-channel bridge. The remaining microscopic obligations are an explicit lawful E8 lift of the full conjugate phase pair and a derivation of the overall nonzero dimensional flux amplitude. Zero remains a covariantly constant section, so phase transport alone does not select the physical flux magnitude.

This is a representation statement only. It does not select a physical Lorentz orbit, a nondegenerate coframe, or a unique healthy spin-2 sector.


Projection/D4 data. The normalized transfer flow, quadratic polarization, and product-duality quarter-turn are exact. The raw projective pair and quadratic Saw plane must not be conflated. Canonically, qSaw is the one normalized componentwise-square step on the complementary shares derived from sawup/sawdown: (u,d)→(u²,d²)/(u²+d²), hence q→2q/(1+q²), Ω→Ω², and rapidity doubles. It is applied only once at the degree-doubling step. Inverse-qSaw is the oriented square-root/projective descent back to the raw shares, and neither qSaw nor its quadratic intensity recovers the independent common Saw sheet sign.


Lorentz-Hodge completion. The physical P/K antisymmetric source currently occupies E35, while the Lorentz Hodge operator supplies the unique covariant E12 partner:
*_L E35 = -E12,    *_L E12 = E35,    *_L² = -1.
For A = α A0 + β *_L A0, det A = α²β²r⁴, so rank four requires both coefficients nonzero. Since End_SO+(3,1)(Λ²) = span_R{I,*_L}, there is no further Lorentz-equivariant linear direction.


Saw–Hodge intertwiner. On the Saw plane V_Saw = span_R{Q,S} and P/K Hodge plane V_PK = span_R{E35,E12}, the quarter-turn and reflection actions agree. Therefore
dim_R Hom_D4(V_Saw,V_PK) = 1,
and every D4-equivariant linear transfer is T_PK = ρ_PK I. In the chosen conventions,
T_PK(Q,S) = ρ_PK(Q E35 + S E12).
Thus the angular form of a Saw-driven rank-completing source is unique, conditional on ρ_PK ≠ 0. This closes form and uniqueness, not activation; the action may still set ρ_PK = 0.


Heavy/Schur mechanism. In a Lorentz-Hodge two-plane, with
B = R_B exp(θ_B J),    D = R_D exp(θ_D J),    J² = -I,
heavy elimination gives
B D⁻¹ B† = (R_B²/R_D) exp[(2θ_B-θ_D)J].
For even D this produces the exact double-angle pair
Q_B = cos(2θ_B) = (b0²-bH²)/(b0²+bH²),
Y_B = sin(2θ_B) = 2b0bH/(b0²+bH²).
However Schur elimination cannot create a mirror-odd/Hodge term from fully even microscopic data: a nonzero β_PK requires an odd bridge current, odd heavy Hessian, or equivalent microscopic odd datum. Likewise G_R-G_L is orientation-sensitive source data, not by itself the real Lorentz-Hodge coefficient β_PK; a lawful real transfer map is still required.


Full-E8 orientation parent. The full-E8 orientation-parent existence obstruction is closed: the scalar Re(a b̄)-E2 pairing vanishes in the shared channels while the pseudoscalar Im(a b̄)-E2 pairing is nonzero in six of seven, including a clean multiplicity-one 146325270 invariant. This establishes representation-theoretic availability of an orientation parent. It does not determine the action coefficient or place the term in the physical P/K constitutive block. The normal condensate Hodge and Lorentz Hodge remain distinct operators.


Activation gate. The PCBF constitutive architecture remains a lawful candidate for placing the Hodge response. But the flat-unsoldered no-go is intact: for a regular local gauge-invariant polynomial four-form built only from scalar selectors, F, DΦ, e, f, and selector projectors, with no background positive-degree form, coframe Hodge, inverse volume, or independent p-form, the vacuum e=f=0, F=0, DΦ=0 has vanishing first variation and vanishing pure-coframe quadratic Hessian. A constant zero-form vacuum therefore does not initiate the needed rank instability.


Current dependency order. The live open gates are:
(i) derive a microscopic nonzero ρ_PK/β_PK transfer;
(ii) supply a lawful positive-degree/coframe activation mechanism;
(iii) prove exterior/soldering rank realization;
(iv) prove a unique healthy physical spin-2 sector.
Only after these are closed may Book 16 promote orientation, vacuum, or cosmological consequences from representation-theoretic possibilities to physical predictions.


Status firewall. Hodge complement available; D4/Saw angular form unique; full-E8 orientation parent available; dynamical coefficient, activation, rank, and healthy spin-2 remain open.

16.I.58 — REAL-FORM ROLE CORRECTION: RAPIDITY AS E8 TRANSPORT AND THE IDEMPOTENT-SEED GATE


Status: NEW EXACT ROLE CORRECTION / PROMOTION OF THE POST-§16.I.57 AUDIT. The literal real Chayet–Garibaldi uplift of the reduced G_C carrier fails, but the same rapidity structure survives as E8(-24) transport of a constitutive idempotent. The reduced one-spin-2 normalization then follows from transported projector geometry. A full A(E8) seed, nonzero radial/flux amplitude, exterior activation, BRST survival, and the healthy complete spectrum remain open.


16.I.58.a — Literal constitutive uplift no-go


On the physical internal-adjoint/dilation I–D plane the reduced construction uses X_ID=sigma_x. The real I–D plane carries opposite Killing signs because the selected internal direction is compact while conformal dilation is noncompact. Hence X_ID is Killing-skew on that physical plane rather than Killing-self-adjoint. Chayet–Garibaldi’s algebra A(e8(-24)) sits inside the Killing-self-adjoint endomorphisms. Therefore no real element of A(e8(-24)) can restrict, or Killing-orthogonally compress, to the nontrivial reduced carrier G_C(r)=exp(r X_ID) for r!=0. Arbitrary transverse 1⊕3875 support cannot repair this necessary compression condition.


Theorem 16.I.58.T1 — Literal G_C Uplift No-Go.
With the current physical I–D identification and X_ID=sigma_x, the nontrivial reduced G_C(r) and its reduced square root are not honest real Chayet–Garibaldi constitutive elements. The §16.I.57 reduced construction remains valid only as a reduced operator until its role is changed.


16.I.58.b — Dilation-generator role correction


Book 16 already has [D,P]=P and [D,K]=-K. Thus on the P/K null doublet ad(D)|_(P/K)=Z, while the inherited P/K Killing pairing is represented by X. Consequently Z^T X=-X Z, so D has exactly the Killing-skew algebraic type required of a Lie-algebra generator. Under the fixed Hadamard change between the null P/K basis and the compact/noncompact basis, X and Z are exchanged. Therefore the sigma_x-shaped rapidity carrier that fails as a constitutive element has the correct type to represent the already-earned dilation/five-grading transport in the alternate basis.


Role-corrected interpretation:
G_C = E8(-24) transport,
not an A(E8) constitutive state.
The constitutive state must instead be an allowed Killing-self-adjoint element transported by this group action.


16.I.58.c — Exact transported-projector theorem


Define P_0=(I+X)/2 and P(w)=exp(-w Z/2) P_0 exp(w Z/2). Direct multiplication gives P(w)=1/2[I+cosh(w) X-sinh(w) J] and P(w)^2=P(w). Using Q=tanh w and S=sech w gives S cosh w=1 and S sinh w=Q. Hence
2 k_0 S P(w)=k_0(S I+X-Q J).
But §16.I.55 already derived H_PK^target=k_0(S I+X-Q J). Therefore


boxed:
H_PK^target=2 k_0 S exp(-w Z/2) P_0 exp(w Z/2).


Theorem 16.I.58.T2 — Transported-Projector Normalization Theorem.
The reduced one-spin-2 coefficient relations h_X=k_0, h_I=k_0 S, h_J=-k_0 Q are the components of one transported primitive idempotent multiplied by one scalar radial normalization. At the reduced P/K level the rank-one condition is protected by idempotency and does not require independent tuning of the I, X, and J coefficients.


Corollary 16.I.58.C1 — Half-rapidity interpretation.
The recurring square-root/half-rapidity factor is the ordinary half-parameter group transport of the selected eigenline/projector. The full rapidity appears in the transported involution, while the half rapidity acts on its primitive projector.


16.I.58.d — Full-E8 seed gate


The transported-projector theorem is not yet a full microscopic lift. If no such seed exists in the bridge-coupled 1⊕3875 sector, the role-corrected projector lift fails even though the reduced transport identity remains exact.


Research Gate 16.I.58.G1 — Idempotent Seed Test.

This replaces the older request to force the reduced G_C matrix itself into A(E8).


16.I.58.e — Local/global unit correction


For any explicitly proved nondegenerate abelian l⊂e8, the Chayet–Garibaldi image H(l) is a Jordan subalgebra with its own local identity e_l, which need not equal the global algebra unit e_A. In the canonical E8 normalization, a normalized Cartan idempotent has counit (h^vee+1)/dim e8=31/248=1/8. Thus a two-axis Cartan Jordan identity has counit 1/4 and cannot equal e_A.


Define q_0=e_A-e_l. Because e_l is idempotent and acts as the identity on H(l), q_0^2=q_0 and q_0 H(l)=0. Therefore R q_0⊕H(l) is an exact unital direct-product completion of the proved local Jordan algebra. This resolves the abstract local/global-unit ambiguity of §16.I.57 but does not by itself identify the physical P/K or I–D seed.


Normalization firewall. The Chayet–Garibaldi Cartan counit 1/8, the benchmark E8 exchange coefficient c_proj=1/8, and the independent relative-singlet normalization Z_ID=(S_rel+2I)/8 remain separately derived. Numerical equality is not yet a proof of one inherited physical normalization.

All are positive for real r. Therefore no sign instability occurs inside this proved Cartan-Jordan sector. Any dangerous transverse sign must arise in the remaining complement of A(E8), where ordinary Jordan fusion is not assumed. This spectrum is CONDITIONAL on the seed lying in the stated Cartan image and is not yet the physical full-E8 spectrum.

Schematically beta_PK^(146)=g_146 kappa_146 Im(a bbar) h_PK(e,f), with kappa_146!=0 representation-theoretically, while the action coupling, pseudoscalar branch, and nonzero exterior rank remain dynamical.

16.I.59 — SINGLE-HYPERBOLA qSAW LIFT, MICROSCOPIC OUTER SQUARE, AND HALF-RAPIDITY PCBF DESCENT


Purpose and dependency. This section formalizes the qSaw amplitude result downstream of the exact Book-7 rapidity hyperbola and the Book-16 role correction G_C = E8 dilation transport rather than an A(E8) constitutive state. It does not reopen the literal G_C∈A(E8) no-go, the §16.I.53 internal rank-four theorem, or the positive-degree/BRST/soldering firewalls.


16.I.59.1 Fixed-radius qSaw lift.


Let
A_+ = |a|²,  A_- = |b|²,
Σ_I = A_+ + A_-,
Δ_I = A_+ - A_-,
μ = 2√(A_+A_-) > 0.
Then identically
Σ_I² - Δ_I² = μ².


For a fixed μ, write
A_+(r) = (μ/2)e^r,  A_-(r) = (μ/2)e^(-r),
so
Σ(r) = μ cosh r,  Δ(r) = μ sinh r,
and Δ/Σ = tanh r.


THEOREM — UNIQUE POSITIVE FIXED-RADIUS LIFT OF qSaw.
Among positive amplitude maps that (i) square the projective odds and (ii) preserve A_+A_- = μ²/4, the lift is uniquely
A_±' = 2A_±²/μ.
Equivalently,
Σ' = (Σ² + Δ²)/μ = (2Σ² - μ²)/μ,
Δ' = 2ΣΔ/μ.
Thus r→2r while μ is invariant, and
Δ'/Σ' = tanh(2r) = qSaw(tanh r).


The inverse map is rapidity halving:
A_±^↓ = √(μA_±/2),
Σ^↓ = √[μ(Σ+μ)/2],
Δ^↓ = sgn(Δ)√[μ(Σ-μ)/2].
Hence the reversible dyadic ladder
… ↔ H_μ(w/2) ↔ H_μ(w) ↔ H_μ(2w) ↔ H_μ(4w) ↔ …
lies on one fixed hyperbola H_μ. qSaw changes the rapidity rung; it does not change the hyperbolic radius.


For the Book-16 microscopic intensity state, the raw Saw state is the w rung and the intensity state is the doubled 2w rung:
Σ_I = μ cosh(2w),
Δ_I = μ sinh(2w),
Π_C = Δ_I/Σ_I = tanh(2w) = qSaw(Q),
with Q = tanh w.


16.I.59.2 Radius/orientation identity on the scalar-null branch.


Write
a conjugate(b) = C_s + iP,
C_s = Re(a conjugate(b)),
P = Im(a conjugate(b)).
Then
μ²/4 = |a|²|b|² = C_s² + P².


On the already-protected scalar-null branch C_s=0,
μ = 2|P|,
P = ε_P μ/2,
ε_P = sgn(P) = ±1.
Therefore the radial magnitude μ and the pseudoscalar magnitude |P| are not independent on this branch. The sign ε_P remains separate orientation data. A convenient state description is (μ, ε_P, w): μ is even radial/Casimir magnitude, ε_P is discrete orientation, and w is the continuous rapidity coordinate on H_μ.


16.I.59.3 Microscopic outer-square theorem.


Define the scalar-null microscopic response matrix
R_I =
[[A_+, P],
 [P,   A_-]].
Because P²=A_+A_-,
det R_I = 0,
and, for nonzero condensate, rank R_I = 1. More strongly,
R_I =
[√A_+, ε_P√A_-]^T [√A_+, ε_P√A_-].


Using
A_+ = (μ/2)e^(2w),
A_- = (μ/2)e^(-2w),
define the half-rapidity amplitude vector
ψ(w) = √(μ/2) [e^w, ε_P e^(-w)]^T.
Then exactly
R_I(2w) = ψ(w)ψ(w)^T.


After normalization by Σ_I,
P_I := R_I/Σ_I
= (1/2)[I + Π_C Z + ε_P(μ/Σ_I)X].
Since
Π_C = tanh(2w) = qSaw(Q),
μ/Σ_I = sech(2w) = c_q(Q),
one has
P_I = (1/2)[I + qSaw(Q)Z + ε_P c_q(Q)X],
with P_I²=P_I and rank P_I=1.


FIREWALL — Xi_C IS NOT THE SAME-HYPERBOLA COMPANION.
The hyperbolic companion of Π_C=tanh(2w) is
c_q(Q)=μ/Σ_I=sech(2w),
not Xi_C=qSaw(S). Xi_C remains a lawful nonlinear D4-completed response readout where previously defined, but it is not the second coordinate of the doubled rapidity hyperbola. No second independent hyperbola is required for the microscopic qSaw lift.


16.I.59.4 Correct inverse-qSaw descent: projective/group square root, not matrix square root.


Because P_I is idempotent, its ordinary matrix square root is itself:
P_I^(1/2)=P_I.
Therefore inverse-qSaw must not be described as an ordinary square root of the projector. More generally, target-only analytic functional calculus cannot rotate the doubled-rung projector eigenspace into the half-rapidity coframe eigenspace.


The lawful square root lives in the already-earned E8 transport. Let
g(r)=exp[-(r/2) ad D].
Then
g(2w)=g(w)²,
so on the inherited real rapidity branch the group-level half transport is canonically g(w).


Put
Π := Π_C = tanh(2w),
c := μ/Σ_I = sech(2w).
Since Π²+c²=1, the exact positive-chart inverse relations are
Q = Π/(1+c) = tanh w,
S = √[2c/(1+c)] = sech w.


Define the descended involution
C_(1/2)(Π,c)
= [Π/(1+c)] Z + ε_P √[2c/(1+c)] X.
Then C_(1/2)²=I and
P_cf = (1/2)[I + C_(1/2)]
= (1/2)[I + QZ + ε_P SX].


Thus inverse-qSaw is the projective shadow of half-rapidity E8 dilation transport. The reduced placement is algebraically closed. What remains open is microscopic action provenance: the parent action must derive this descended C_(1/2) from the condensate/transport data rather than accept C as an independent constitutive input.


16.I.59.5 Canonical Phi_2 + Hodge source and the A4 normalization criterion.


In the certified physical P/K quotient, the canonical bracket-recoupled map has pure-intensity coefficients
Phi_2[|a|²]|_(P/K) = -(4/7)L_-,
Phi_2[|b|²]|_(P/K) = -(4/7)L_+.
After lowering with the invariant P/K pairing X,
X Phi_(2,A) = -(4/7) diag(A_+,A_-).


The already-earned normal Hodge/pseudoscalar completion carries the same absolute internal Clebsch magnitude 4/7, with the fixed sign determined by the established Hodge/P-K orientation convention. Hence the completed source has the form
K_Phi2 = -(4/7)
[[A_+, ε_H P],
 [ε_H P, A_-]],
with ε_H=±1 fixed by convention. Therefore
det K_Phi2 = (16/49)(A_+A_- - P²) = 0
on the scalar-null branch.


If the action weights the amplitude and pseudoscalar legs independently,
K_action = -(4/7)
[[g_A A_+, g_P ε_H P],
 [g_P ε_H P, g_A A_-]],
then
det K_action = (4μ²/49)(g_A²-g_P²).
For μ≠0,
rank K_action = 1  iff  |g_P|=|g_A|.


INTERPRETATION.
The common-normalization condition is therefore best read as preservation of an already-existing microscopic outer square. The remaining A4 question is not whether two unrelated representation coefficients happen to coincide; it is whether one lawful parent invariant transmits the factorized source into the BF kernel with one coefficient.


The exact dilation overlap also satisfies
N_3875(D)=-(12/7)Σ_I
= 3 tr(K_Phi2)
in the common source normalization. This factor-three relation is algebraic only; it does not identify independent action couplings.


16.I.59.6 Heavy-field rank protection.


For
S_PCBF = ∫[B^T∧KF -(1/2)B^T∧MB + L_alg],
assume the descended physical BF map has one P/K image line:
K = u p^T,
where P_cf=pp^T and u may live in an arbitrarily large heavy/auxiliary B-space. If M is invertible on the eliminated sector, the Schur complement is
K^T M^(-1) K
= (u^T M^(-1)u) pp^T
= k_eff P_cf.
Therefore arbitrary nonsingular heavy-sector mixing cannot generate a second P/K eigenline; it can only change the overall scalar coefficient. Rank one is protected once the BF coupling itself has a one-dimensional physical image.


This protection does not perform inverse-qSaw. If the BF map is built from P_I(2w), Gaussian elimination leaves its physical image proportional to P_I(2w); it does not produce P_cf(w). The half-rapidity descent must occur before, or as part of, the BF coupling map.


16.I.59.7 Radial activation and the remaining amplitude gate.


The exact dilation overlap becomes
N_3875(D)=-(12/7) μ cosh(2w).
For a fixed topological sector n=∫b∧b, the admitted PhiBB term therefore has the schematic condensate dependence
S_PhiBB = -(12/7) g_PhiBB n μ cosh(2w),
up to the fixed action/orientation conventions.


qSaw preserves μ and therefore does not select a nonzero radius. If the algebraic parent supplies a radial potential V_0(μ), finite-radius stationarity reduces to the scalar equation
V_0'(μ_*) = (12/7) g_PhiBB n cosh(2w)
up to the overall action sign, followed by the appropriate second-variation test. The existence, sign, and normalization of g_PhiBB, the nontrivial global sector, and finite-amplitude stabilization remain dynamical questions.


16.I.59.8 Status and firewalls.


CLOSED / EXACT at the stated reduced or source level:
(1) one fixed-radius hyperbola H_μ underlies the reversible qSaw ladder;

(7) inverse-qSaw is not P_I^(1/2); it is the projective shadow of half-rapidity E8 transport;
(8) the canonical Phi_2 amplitude/Hodge source has rank one before independent action weights are introduced;
(9) nonsingular heavy-field elimination preserves a one-dimensional physical BF image.


OPEN / NOT PROMOTED:

(6) the complete constrained/heavy spectrum with exactly one healthy low-energy spin-2 combination;
(7) absolute mirror selection and physical cosmology.


The live A4 chain is therefore:
scalar-null condensate
→ (Π_C, μ/Σ_I)=(tanh2w,sech2w)
→ half-rapidity E8 transport / inverse-qSaw

All arrows through P_cf are algebraically fixed. The remaining A4 action gate is the single-parent transmission into K, while μ≠0 remains the separate radial/activation gate.
16.I.60 — PROJECTIVE ALGEBRAIC FULL-E8 LIFT AND THE A4 SINGLET-CONTAMINATION REDUCTION


Purpose and dependency. This section combines the §16.I.59 single-hyperbola/inverse-qSaw theorem with an already-earned full-E8 Chayet–Garibaldi transport construction in the active research record. It sharpens A4 without reopening the §16.I.58 real-form/idempotent no-go. In particular, the element E_0 below has the required rank-one restriction on the physical P/K label plane but is not thereby asserted to be a global idempotent of A(e8(-24)).


16.I.60.1 Canonical balanced P/K spectral subtraction.


Let
Y_mu = P_mu - K_mu,
W_- = eta^(mu nu) Y_mu odot Y_nu.
For the already-computed Chayet–Garibaldi symmetric-adjoint map S,
S(W_-)|_(P/K) =
[[360,-240],
 [-240,360]].
The label eigenvalues are
lambda_+ = 120 on P+K,
lambda_- = 600 on P-K.


For a general two-term element
E_(alpha,beta) = alpha S(W_-) + beta e_A,
the corresponding P/K eigenvalues are
120 alpha + beta
and
600 alpha + beta.
Demanding annihilation of the unwanted P-K label line therefore fixes uniquely, up to an overall scalar,
beta/alpha = -600.
Hence define
E_0 := S(W_-) - 600 e_A,
for which
E_0|_(P/K) = -480 P_+,
P_+ = (I+X)/2.
The fixed -480 coefficient is the previously derived E8 value -16 hvee.


THEOREM — UNIQUE CANONICAL TWO-TERM RANK-ONE P/K COMBINATION.
Inside span{S(W_-),e_A}, the projective combination with zero P-K eigenvalue is uniquely [1:-600]. This is a reduced spectral statement. It does not imply E_0 star E_0 = E_0 globally.


16.I.60.2 Full-E8 transport to the Saw/coframe projector.


Let the earned dilation transport act by
E(w) = exp[-(w/2) ad D] · E_0.
Because the E8 action preserves A(e8(-24)),
E(w)|_(P/K)
= -480 exp[-wZ/2] P_+ exp[wZ/2]
= -240 [[1,e^(-w)],[e^w,1]].


Using the normalized P/K invariant pairing G_PK=X and S(w)=sech w, define the bilinear lift
Kcal_w := -[S(w)/480] G_PK E(w).
Then
Kcal_w|_(P/K)
= (S/2) [[e^w,1],[1,e^(-w)]]
= (1/2)[I + QZ + SX],
where Q=tanh w.
With the inherited orientation sign,
Kcal_w|_(P/K)
= P_cf(w,epsilon_P)
= (1/2)[I + QZ + epsilon_P S X].
Therefore the full-E8 algebraic lift of the descended rank-one P/K projector is already available.


FIREWALL.
This closes existence of a full-E8 transported bilinear with the correct physical restriction. It does not prove that the microscopic PCBF action places Kcal_w in its K-kernel with nonzero coefficient, nor does it control all transverse eigenvalues or the complete constrained spectrum.


16.I.60.3 Algebraic inverse-qSaw coordinate and projective degree ladder.


Put
t=e^w>0.
Because the microscopic intensity rung is at 2w,
A_+/A_- = e^(4w),
so t is determined algebraically by
A_- t^4 - A_+ = 0.


The raw Saw coordinates are
Q = (t^2-1)/(t^2+1),
S = 2t/(t^2+1),
and
qSaw(Q) = (t^4-1)/(t^4+1) = Pi_C.


The descended projector is
P_cf(t,epsilon_P)
= 1/(1+t^2)
[[t^2,epsilon_P t],
 [epsilon_P t,1]],
which is manifestly rank one.


More globally write t=r/s. Then
P_cf(r,s,epsilon_P)
= 1/(r^2+s^2)
[[r^2,epsilon_P rs],
 [epsilon_P rs,s^2]].
Only [r:s] in RP1 matters. The raw complementary shares are projectively [r^2:s^2], while the microscopic intensity shares are [r^4:s^4]. Hence
[r:s] -> [r^2:s^2] -> [r^4:s^4]
is the exact projective degree ladder, with qSaw the degree-doubling map from the raw share rung to the microscopic intensity rung.


16.I.60.4 Finite graded full-E8 realization.


Because E_0 is generated from a quadratic P/K carrier, decompose it into D-grades
E_0 = E_(+2) + E_(0) + E_(-2).
Then the transported element is the finite Laurent expression
E(t) = t^(-1) E_(+2) + E_(0) + t E_(-2).


After multiplying by the Saw normalization,
Kcal(t)
= -1/[240(1+t^2)] G [E_(+2) + t E_(0) + t^2 E_(-2)].
Thus the half-rapidity bridge does not require a transcendental constitutive dependence on this finite graded sector. In homogeneous projective coordinates,
Kcal(r,s)
= -1/[240(r^2+s^2)] G
[s^2 E_(+2) + rs E_(0) + r^2 E_(-2)].
Its physical P/K restriction is exactly P_cf(r,s).


16.I.60.5 A4 contamination theorem.


Once the canonical transported channel is available, the most general reduced addition of an independent invariant identity/BF channel has the form
K_red = g_0 I + g_1 P_cf.
Since P_cf has eigenvalues 1 and 0,
K_red has eigenvalues
g_0+g_1
and
g_0,
so
det K_red = g_0(g_0+g_1).


For the desired physical P_cf branch,
rank K_red = 1
iff
g_0=0 and g_1!=0,
assuming a finite nonsingular scalar heavy kernel on both label directions.


Equivalently, for the older parametrization
K=aI+bC,
C=2P_cf-I,
one has
K=(a+b)P_cf+(a-b)(I-P_cf).
Hence the old condition a=b is exactly the statement that the orthogonal identity contamination coefficient vanishes.


This is a stronger reduction of A4: the unresolved issue is no longer discovery of a full-E8 lift or equality of two microscopic response Clebsches. It is whether the microscopic action activates the canonical Kcal_w channel without an independent (I-P_cf) contribution, or else removes that orthogonal channel by a genuine constraint/singular-heavy mechanism.


16.I.60.6 Symmetry and boundary no-go for automatic removal.


D4 covariance alone cannot force g_0=0 because I is itself D4 invariant. Likewise a requirement that the PCBF source vanish at the unsoldered/radial origin does not suffice: a contaminating coefficient may have the form
g_0(mu)=mu f(mu,...)
and vanish at mu=0 while remaining nonzero at a selected mu_*.


Therefore eliminating the orthogonal channel requires additional action structure: a single-parent selection rule, a representation/character distinction not shared by I, a constraint that removes the orthogonal label direction, a singular/infinite-heavy limit on that direction, or an equivalent mechanism.
