D. Exact sign audit of the integrated gravitational 4-forms


Write the three selected-line forms as


H_4:=R_{ab}∧e^a∧e^b,


E_4:=ε_{abcd}R^{ab}∧e^c∧e^d,


V_4:=ε_{abcd}e^a∧e^b∧e^c∧e^d.


For the Holst form all internal Lorentz indices are contracted with the metric. Therefore the improper frame transformation contributes no determinant sign:


H_4' = p^*H_4.


Since p reverses the orientation of M,


∫_M p^*H_4
=
-∫_M H_4.


Hence


boxed(∫H_4→-∫H_4).


For the Einstein–Cartan form the internal Levi-Civita tensor contributes detΛ=-1:


E_4'=-p^*E_4.


The base orientation reversal contributes the second minus sign upon integration, so


boxed(∫E_4→+∫E_4).


Likewise


V_4'=-p^*V_4,


and therefore


boxed(∫V_4→+∫V_4).


Thus the integrated-form character is exactly


(∫H_4,∫E_4,∫V_4)
→
(-∫H_4,+∫E_4,+∫V_4).


This is the same character as the derived coefficient vector.


E. Exact selected-line gravity covariance


The earned selected-line gravity action is


S_grav
=
C_H∫H_4
+
C_EC∫E_4
+
Λ_C∫V_4.


Under the orientation-compensated q realization,


C_H∫H_4
→
(-C_H)(-∫H_4)
=
C_H∫H_4,


C_EC∫E_4
→
C_EC∫E_4,


Λ_C∫V_4
→
Λ_C∫V_4.


Therefore


boxed(S_grav[q·Φ]=S_grav[Φ])


for the transformed polarization/background data.


Theorem 16.I.99.T1 — Orientation-compensated selected-line gravity pass.


At the already-earned quarter-Y branch, the complete Holst–Einstein–Cartan–volume selected-line gravitational action is exactly q-covariant provided the physical Lorentz reflection in q is globally realized together with an orientation-reversing spacetime map and the polarization coefficients transform in their certified Y-reflection representation.


The matching is not accidental:


Holst coefficient: odd,
Holst integral: odd;


Einstein–Cartan coefficient: even,
Einstein–Cartan integral: even;


cosmological coefficient: even,
volume integral: even.


All three products are q-even.


F. Internal-only reflection fails the same sign test


If one applies the improper Lorentz/frame transformation Λ while leaving the oriented base manifold fixed, then there is no degree-minus-one pullback sign. The form characters become


∫H_4→+∫H_4,


∫E_4→-∫E_4,


∫V_4→-∫V_4.


These are the opposite of the coefficient characters required above. Generically,


C_H∫H_4,
C_EC∫E_4,
Λ_C∫V_4


would then all be odd rather than invariant.


Corollary 16.I.99.C1 — Base-orientation reversal is required for the earned gravity realization of q.


Within the present selected-line action and the certified Y characters of its coefficients, the q symmetry cannot be represented merely as an internal improper Lorentz transformation. The spacetime realization must carry the compensating orientation reversal, or some additional orientation-odd background structure not presently in the manuscript would have to replace it.


This turns the spacetime-orientation choice from a convention into part of the physical definition of the candidate q symmetry.


G. The rank-one spin-2 projector survives q


The earlier gravity analysis established a D4-covariant parent family with one projected coframe


E_F^a=n_F(u)^T𝓔^a,


invariant under simultaneous orthogonal label transport. The healthy kinetic matrix satisfies the rank-one condition


det K_grav=0,


and at the quarter-Y balance branch the relevant rank-one projector is fixed projectively by the Y reflection.


Consequently q does not require a second independent coframe kinetic line. It acts within the D4-covariant rank-one family and preserves the selected projective kinetic direction at the balance branch.


Theorem 16.I.99.T2 — q compatibility with one-spin-2 rank-one gravity.


At the quarter-Y balance branch, the pair-level Y reflection induced by q preserves the already-earned rank-one coframe kinetic projector projectively. Therefore promoting q to the orientation-compensated gravitational symmetry above does not reopen the rejected two-spin-2 completion and does not require the orthogonal coframe line to acquire a kinetic term.


This is important: the old full-quarter-turn obstruction and the present reflection symmetry are not the same requirement. The reflection can preserve the healthy rank-one line even though a generic full D4-selected vacuum need not preserve every generator.


H. Principal odd kinetic sector


Section 16.I.96 already established that q exchanges the two D-grade Weyl kinetic corridors. Let the corresponding principal kinetic normalizations be


Z_+,


Z_-.


Under q the two kinetic operators are exchanged. With the selected Spin(10) connection and the coframe/connection transformed covariantly under the orientation-compensated parity action, principal kinetic invariance requires


boxed(Z_+=Z_-)


unless an additional q-odd background factor compensates the exchange.


Theorem 16.I.99.T3 — Principal odd-kinetic q criterion.


For the presently specified doubled D=±1 fermion sector and in the absence of an additional compensating transformed background, the principal fermion kinetic action is q-invariant if and only if


Z_+=Z_-.


At that point the two grade corridors form the vectorlike Dirac-16 structure already identified in §16.I.97, and the ordinary paired perturbative measure cancellation is available under a q-symmetric regulator.


Firewall. The equality Z_+=Z_- is a required symmetry relation, not yet a derived dynamical equality of the bosonic parent. Therefore the fermionic kinetic pass remains CONDITIONAL rather than exact at the microscopic theory level.


I. Selector parity: compatible source structure, broken selected vacuum


The certified bosonic grade reversal is


n_+↔-n_-,


D→-D.


The corresponding linear selector source obeys


J_D→-J_D,


while quadratic expressions in J_D are even. Therefore the basic source contraction


J_D D


is q-even, and the already-derived source parity is compatible with action covariance.


This does not yet certify the complete selector action. A complete proof would require the full selector potential, kinetic terms, measure, and all couplings to the remaining sectors to be written in one q-covariant form.


For any q-even selector potential with degenerate nonzero minima ±D_0,


q:D_0↔-D_0.


Thus choosing one nonzero vacuum spontaneously breaks q unless the two extrema are identified by a larger gauge redundancy.


Theorem 16.I.99.T4 — Action/vacuum split for selector reversal.


The known selector source parity is compatible with q covariance, but a selected D≠0 vacuum is not q-fixed in general. Exact q covariance of the parent action is therefore logically compatible with spontaneous q breaking by vacuum selection.


This is the correct framework for the mirror-grade question: if q exchanges the two grade orientations, an infrared theory containing only one orientation cannot retain q as an ordinary unbroken global symmetry unless the exchanged sectors are gauge-identified.


J. Bridge, Yukawa, and lower-order odd interactions remain the hard action gate


The manuscript has not yet supplied enough independent coefficient data and transformation laws to certify all bridge/Yukawa/pair interactions under q. In particular, the primitive q construction is a representation lift whose same-grade pair action is ±Q_Y; it has not been proved to be a full E8(-24) algebra automorphism acting on every bosonic and odd interaction vertex.


Therefore one may not infer complete interaction invariance merely from the inherited E8 bracket or from the pair-level Q_Y action.


For every independent interaction tensor T_A with coupling y_A, the required audit is


T_A(qψ_i,qψ_j,…;q·B)
=
χ_A T_A(ψ_i,ψ_j,…;B),


with


y_A→χ_A y_A,


χ_A=±1,


or, for a multiplet of couplings, the corresponding finite-dimensional q representation. Fixed couplings with χ_A=-1 would be an action-level obstruction unless they arise from q-odd condensates/backgrounds.


Current status:


inherited tree-level bracket structure: known;


primitive pair action ±Q_Y: known;


complete bridge/Yukawa coefficient representation under q: NOT YET DERIVED;


complete lower-order odd interaction invariance: OPEN.


This is now the narrowest remaining local action-level symmetry gate.


K. Consequence for the global anomaly problem


The orientation-compensated realization gives a concrete spacetime parity-like meaning to the disconnected primitive q class, while §16.I.98 fixes


q²=+I


and the finite fermionic dihedral double cover. But a bordism or η-invariant computation is still premature until the symmetry of the complete microscopic action is known.


The correct ordering is now


1. close the bridge/Yukawa and selector-action q audit;


2. determine whether the full bosonic/fermionic transformation embeds in an E8(-24) normalizer or only in a post-soldering disconnected extension;


3. specify the selected-vacuum realization and whether q is unbroken, spontaneously broken, or gauged/identified;


4. only then formulate the correct twisted five-dimensional bordism/η problem.

§16.I.100 — Clifford-Normalizer No-Go for the Quarter Dressing, No Full-E8 Extension of the Exact Primitive q, and the Surviving Post-Soldering Symmetry Gate


Status. EXACT SPIN-LIE-ALGEBRA NORMALIZER NO-GO FOR R_D AND q ON THE COMPLETE ODD 128; EXACT NO FULL E8(-24) LIE-ALGEBRA AUTOMORPHISM EXTENDING THE §16.I.96 q ON THAT COMPLETE 128; EARLIER g_X FULL-E8 EXCHANGE REMAINS VALID AND DISTINCT; AUTOMATIC INHERITED-BRACKET q COVARIANCE CLOSED NEGATIVELY; POST-SOLDERING REDUCED-ACTION q SYMMETRY AND MODIFIED FULL-E8-COMPATIBLE PRIMITIVE LIFTS REMAIN OPEN.


Sections 16.I.96–16.I.99 established an exact primitive involution q whose pair descent is ±Q_Y, an order-16 fermionic dihedral closure, and a viable orientation-compensated action on the earned gravitational line. The remaining hope was that this exact q might also lie in, or normalize, the Spin(12,4) structure underlying the 120⊕128 realization of E8(-24). If so, inherited E8 interactions would acquire q covariance automatically rather than term by term.


That hope fails for the exact primitive q already constructed. The obstruction is not a matter of choosing a different Lorentz representative ℓ. It comes from the quarter dressing R_D itself. The manuscript definitions are sufficient to make the obstruction representation-theoretically exact.


A. Clifford provenance of the quarter generator


Book 16 fixes the conformal-normal grading element as


D=a∧b,


with its action on the odd spinorial carrier denoted


Z:=ad D.


The same manuscript defines J_I as the real-linear endomorphism of the odd spinor carrier induced by the oriented internal ten-dimensional Clifford volume element. On the internal real chiral spinor,


J_I²=-I.


On the D=±1 odd bundle,


K:=ZJ_I,


K²=-I,


and


R_D
=
exp[(π/4)K]
=
(1/√2)(I+K).


Let the sixteen-dimensional vector space underlying Spin(12,4) be decomposed as


V=L_4⊕W_12,


where L_4 is the physical Lorentz four-plane and


W_12=I_10⊕N_2


is the internal ten-plane together with the conformal-normal plane spanned by a,b.


Because Z is the spin action of the oriented normal bivector a∧b and J_I is the action of the oriented internal ten-volume, their product K is, up to the already-fixed common normalization/sign convention, the spin action of the oriented twelve-dimensional Clifford volume element of W_12.


This provenance matters. K is not an arbitrary complex structure on the 128; it is a definite higher-grade Clifford operator.


B. Chiral-safe normalizer test


A naive test on Clifford vectors is suggestive but is not by itself sufficient, because the even Clifford algebra acting on one chiral spin module has Hodge-dual identifications. The decisive test should therefore be made directly on the Lie algebra representation


ρ:so(12,4)→End(S),


S≅128.


Choose nonzero orthogonal vectors


v∈L_4,


w∈W_12,


and form the mixed bivector


X:=vw∈Λ²V≅so(12,4).


The twelve-volume K commutes with v, because v lies outside W_12 and passes through twelve W factors with even parity. It anticommutes with w, because the volume element of an even-dimensional W anticommutes with each W vector. Hence


KX=-XK.


Using


R_D=(I+K)/√2,


R_D^{-1}=(I-K)/√2,


one obtains exactly


R_D X R_D^{-1}
=
(1/2)(I+K)X(I-K)
=
-XK.


The right-hand side has Clifford degree twelve: X contributes one physical vector and one W vector, while multiplication by the W twelve-volume contracts the repeated W vector and leaves one physical factor times an eleven-form in W.


On a sixteen-dimensional chiral spin module, a degree-twelve Clifford action is Hodge-dual to a degree-four action, because the total sixteen-volume acts as the fixed chirality scalar. Therefore the transformed operator belongs to the Λ⁴V operator sector rather than the Λ²V sector.


After complexification these sectors are inequivalent Spin(16,C) modules:


Λ²16 has dimension 120,


Λ⁴16 has dimension 1820.


Thus, for the mixed bivector X,


boxed(
R_D ρ(X) R_D^{-1}
∉
ρ(so(12,4))
).


Theorem 16.I.100.T1 — Quarter-dressing spin-normalizer no-go.


The exact quarter dressing


R_D=exp[(π/4)ZJ_I]


does not normalize the Spin(12,4) Lie-algebra action on the complete chiral 128. In particular,


R_D∉N_{GL(128,R)}(ρ(Spin(12,4))),


and therefore R_D is not the spin representation of a Spin(12,4) element.


The obstruction is witnessed already by any nonzero mixed physical/W bivector. Its 120 action is conjugated into the 1820 Clifford-operator sector.


This theorem is stronger than the earlier observation that no obvious bivector exponent generates R_D. It rules out the full representation normalizer, not merely the connected spin image.


C. The exact primitive q also fails the Spin(12,4) normalizer test


The primitive lift is


q=R_D𝒫_ℓ,


where


𝒫_ℓ=ρ(r_ℓr_a)


is induced by a genuine even Clifford/Spin transformation. Hence 𝒫_ℓ normalizes the Spin(12,4) Lie algebra representation.


Suppose q normalized that representation. Then


R_D=q𝒫_ℓ^{-1}


would be the product of two normalizer elements and would itself normalize the representation, contradicting Theorem 16.I.100.T1.


Corollary 16.I.100.C1 — Exact primitive-q Spin-normalizer no-go.


The §16.I.96 primitive involution


q=R_D𝒫_ℓ


does not normalize the Spin(12,4) Lie-algebra action on the complete odd 128.


This conclusion is independent of the Lorentz representative ℓ within the gauge-canonical class [q]. Changing ℓ by connected Lorentz conjugacy cannot remove an obstruction carried by R_D.


D. Surjectivity of the E8 odd-odd bracket


The live E8 decomposition has


e8(-24)=120⊕128


with the odd-odd bracket


B:Λ²128→120.


Book 16 already establishes


Λ²128=120⊕8008,


8008=ker B.


Therefore B has rank


8008+120-8008=120,


and is surjective:


boxed(Im B=120).


Equivalently,


[128,128]=120.


This elementary point is the missing bridge from the spin-normalizer obstruction to the full-E8 automorphism question.


E. No full-E8 automorphism can extend the exact q on the complete 128


Assume, for contradiction, that there exists a Lie-algebra automorphism


A∈Aut(e8(-24))


whose restriction to the complete odd subspace is exactly


A|_128=q.


Because q maps the 128 to itself, A preserves the odd subspace. By bracket equivariance,


A([s,t])=[qs,qt]


for all s,t∈128. Since


[128,128]=120,


it follows that


A(120)=120.


Now let X∈120 and s∈128. The E8 bracket gives the spin-module action


[X,s]=ρ(X)s.


Applying A,


qρ(X)s
=
A([X,s])
=
[A(X),q s]
=
ρ(A(X))q s.


Hence


qρ(X)q^{-1}=ρ(A(X))∈ρ(120).


So q would have to normalize the 120 spin action.


But Corollary 16.I.100.C1 proves that it does not. Contradiction.


Theorem 16.I.100.T2 — No full-E8(-24) automorphism extension of the exact primitive q.


There is no Lie-algebra automorphism of e8(-24) whose action on the complete odd 128 is the exact primitive involution q constructed in §16.I.96.


Equivalently, the hoped-for route


exact q on 128
→
automatic extension to 120
→
automatic covariance of all inherited E8 brackets


is CLOSED NEGATIVELY.


This is an exact representation-theoretic no-go, not a failure to find a convenient representative.


F. This does not invalidate the earlier genuine full-E8 exchange g_X


Book 16 §16.I.60.12 constructed a different exchange operation. With r_a the normal reflection and r_I an odd internal compensating reflection product,


g_X=r_Ir_a∈Spin(12,4).


Its induced spinor action and adjoint action therefore preserve the full E8 bracket by construction. It reverses the conformal grading and exchanges the physical P/K sectors, but it is noncanonical because of the internal compensator choice.


The present theorem does not contradict or weaken that result.


The distinction is now sharp:


FULL-E8 EXCHANGE g_X:
Spin(12,4)-compatible, bracket-compatible, but internally compensated/noncanonical and not equal to the exact product-duality primitive q.


PRODUCT-DUALITY PRIMITIVE q:
gauge-canonical at the Lorentz-representative level, exact q²=1, exact pair descent ±Q_Y, but not a Spin(12,4) normalizer and therefore not extendable as a full-E8 automorphism on the complete 128.


These are two different discrete structures and must no longer be conflated.


G. Consequence for inherited interactions


The no-go removes a tempting shortcut. One may not infer q covariance of an odd interaction merely because that interaction descends from the inherited E8 bracket or adjoint action.


For the normal-connection exchange of §16.I.94, the inherited bracket factorization still proves its separate tree-level statement:


the physical 8008⊂30380 current vanishes before the positive selector metric is applied, so inherited N_20 exchange does not regenerate the forbidden 30380 contact at tree level.


That result remains valid.


What fails is the additional inference


inherited E8 interaction
⇒
q-invariant interaction.


The exact q is not an E8 automorphism, so q characters of the reduced post-soldering interaction terms must be audited directly.


Book 16 presently contains no fully declared independent Yukawa vertex with specified tensor and coefficient data. Accordingly there is no honest Yukawa q-parity theorem to prove or falsify at this stage. Any future Yukawa or nonminimal selector vertex must be tested when it is actually defined.


H. What survives of q


The no-go is substantial but not fatal to the post-soldering program.


Already-established q results survive unchanged:


• q²=I on the complete real odd carrier;


• the primitive order-16 fermionic dihedral representation and order-eight D4 pair quotient;


• gauge-canonicity of [q] modulo connected Lorentz frame choice;


• pair descent Λ²q=±Q_Y on the certified physical normal pair;


• orientation-compensated covariance of the earned selected-line gravitational action;


• projective preservation of the healthy rank-one one-spin-2 projector at quarter-Y balance;


• conditional principal odd kinetic covariance when Z_+=Z_-;


• finite-mode determinant +1 and the paired local perturbative measure cancellation under a q-symmetric regulator.


What is lost is only the claim that these structures arise from one full-E8 automorphism acting as the exact q on every odd direction.


Two logically distinct research paths remain.


Path A — Reduced post-soldering q symmetry.


Treat q as an emergent/disconnected symmetry of the selected reduced action after soldering. Audit each surviving interaction tensor and coefficient directly. The full pre-soldered E8 action need not possess q.


Path B — Modified full-E8-compatible primitive lift.


Search for a different primitive operator q' whose same-grade action on the certified physical normal pair still equals ±Q_Y but whose action away from that physical pair corridor is modified so that q' lies in the Spin(12,4) normalizer or extends to an E8 automorphism.


Theorem 16.I.100.T2 rules out only an extension of the exact §16.I.96 q on the complete 128. It does not prove uniqueness of all primitive lifts with the same physical pair quotient.


I. Consequence for the global-anomaly program


The global-anomaly problem must now be phrased more carefully.


If Path A is correct, the relevant disconnected symmetry group is a symmetry group of the post-soldering reduced theory, not a global symmetry of the full E8 parent. Its bordism/η problem must be built from the actual reduced field content and actual surviving q-covariant interactions.


If Path B succeeds, then a different full-E8-compatible primitive lift would define a different global symmetry group and potentially a different anomaly problem.


Therefore a five-dimensional η-invariant calculation for the present exact q remains premature until the theory chooses between these two paths and closes the corresponding action-level symmetry gate.


J. Updated live frontier


EXACT / CLOSED


• J_I has certified internal ten-volume Clifford provenance.


• Z is the certified spin action generated by the normal bivector D=a∧b.


• K=ZJ_I is therefore the internal-plus-normal twelve-volume action up to the fixed manuscript normalization/sign convention.

• B:Λ²128→120 is surjective because Λ²128=120⊕8008 and ker B=8008.

§16.I.101 — Grassmannian-Duality No-Go for Every Full-E8-Compatible Primitive Lift of Q_Y


Status. EXACT CLASSIFICATION OF FULL-E8-COMPATIBLE SAME-CHIRALITY ODD LIFTS IN THE AUDITED 120⊕128 PARENT; EXACT EXTERIOR-POWER / GRASSMANNIAN NO-GO FOR THE REQUIRED FIVE-FORM PRODUCT DUALITY; §16.I.100 PATH B CLOSED NEGATIVELY; REDUCED POST-SOLDERING q PATH REMAINS THE ONLY LIVE PRODUCT-DUALITY ROUTE.


Section 16.I.100 proved that the exact primitive involution q=R_D𝒫_ℓ does not normalize the Spin(12,4) action and therefore cannot extend to a full e8(-24) automorphism. It deliberately left open a narrower loophole: perhaps a different q′, modified away from the certified physical pair corridor, could be full-E8 compatible while inducing the same pair quotient


Λ²q′|_normal = ±Q_Y,


Q_Y=*₁₀⊗R_prod.


The present section closes that loophole for every automorphism preserving the already-audited odd 128 subspace. The obstruction is geometric and does not depend on the particular quarter dressing of §16.I.96.


A. Full-E8 compatibility forces a same-chirality orthogonal-spin lift


Write the live parent decomposition as


e8(-24)=120⊕128,


with


120≅so(12,4),


and let S denote the real chiral odd module, dim_R S=128. The inherited odd-odd bracket is


B:Λ²S→120,


with


Λ²S=120⊕8008,


ker B=8008.


Hence B is surjective and


[S,S]=120.


Suppose A∈Aut(e8(-24)) preserves S and let


q′:=A|_S.


Surjectivity immediately gives


A(120)=A([S,S])=[S,S]=120.


Thus


φ:=A|_120


is an automorphism of so(12,4), and bracket covariance of the even-odd action gives


q′ρ(X)q′⁻¹=ρ(φ X)


for all X∈so(12,4).


The complexified algebra is type D₈. Its nontrivial diagram automorphism exchanges the two half-spin highest weights and therefore exchanges the two chiral 128-dimensional half-spin modules. It cannot be implemented by an invertible endomorphism S→S of one fixed real chirality. Consequently any φ occurring in the displayed same-chirality intertwining equation is represented on the vector space V≅R^{12,4} by an orthogonal transformation G with an even Clifford lift acting within S. Connectedness of G is irrelevant here; only preservation of the chosen half-spin module matters.


Choose one such same-chirality Clifford lift ρ(ĝ). Then


T:=ρ(ĝ)⁻¹q′


commutes with ρ(so(12,4)). For signature (12,4), p−q≡0 mod 8, and the real chiral half-spin module is absolutely real irreducible. Equivalently, the corresponding simple component of the even real Clifford algebra is Mat(128,R). Therefore its real commutant is only


End_{so(12,4)}(S)=R·I.


Hence


q′=λρ(ĝ)


for some nonzero real λ.


Now use the odd-odd bracket. Since B is nonzero and surjective,


B(q′s,q′t)=A(B(s,t))


and Spin/orthogonal equivariance of B gives


λ² Ad_G B(s,t)=Ad_G B(s,t).


Therefore


λ²=1.


Theorem 16.I.101.T1 — Full-E8-compatible primitive-lift classification.


Within the audited decomposition e8(-24)=so(12,4)⊕S with one fixed real chiral S≅128, every Lie-algebra automorphism preserving S acts on S as


q′=±ρ(ĝ),


where ĝ is an even Clifford lift of an orthogonal vector transformation G preserving that same half-spin chirality. There is no additional exotic real-linear same-chirality normalizer available to repair the physical pair action.


The overall sign disappears on Λ²S and is irrelevant for the pair quotient.


B. The 8008 pair sector is the six-form representation


Book 16 already identifies


8008≅Λ⁶V,


V≅R^{12,4}.


Therefore the action induced by any full-E8-compatible q′ of Theorem 16.I.101.T1 on the 8008 sector is exactly the six-form action


Λ⁶G.


This turns the primitive-lift problem into a vector/exterior-power problem. No freedom remains to alter the 8008 action independently while keeping q′ the restriction of a full-E8 automorphism.


C. The certified five-form normal corridor inside Λ⁶V


Use the already-fixed orthogonal splitting


V=L₄⊕I₁₀⊕N₂,


where I₁₀ is the internal Euclidean ten-plane and N₂ is the conformal-normal two-plane. Book 16 has independently established


126⊕bar126 = (Λ⁵I₁₀)_C


as the ±i Hodge decomposition of the real 252-dimensional internal five-form space, and the conformal vector decomposition contains the two normal lines of grades ±2.


Accordingly the complete real same-grade five-form normal pair is the concrete six-form subspace


W_N:=Λ⁵I₁₀⊗N₂ ⊂ Λ⁶V,


embedded by


α⊗n ↦ α∧n.


On this subspace the already-earned heavy product-duality involution is


T_Y(α∧n)
:=
(*₁₀α)∧(R_prod n),


with


*₁₀²=-I,


R_prod²=-I,


T_Y²=I.


Thus a full-E8-compatible primitive lift would require


Λ⁶G|_{W_N}=±T_Y.


D. Exterior-power support test


Assume for contradiction that some invertible vector map G satisfies the last equation. The following argument needs only G∈GL(V), so it is stronger than the orthogonal requirement inherited from full E8.


Let A⊂I₁₀ be any five-dimensional internal plane. Choose a nonzero decomposable α∈Λ⁵A, and let ℓ⊂N₂ be any normal line with nonzero n∈ℓ. The decomposable six-form α∧n has support plane


A⊕ℓ.


Because G is invertible, Λ⁶G sends it to a decomposable six-form with support


G(A⊕ℓ).


In Euclidean dimension ten, the Hodge dual of a decomposable five-form spanning A is again decomposable and spans the orthogonal complement A^⊥. Therefore T_Y(α∧n) has support


A^⊥⊕R_prodℓ.


Equality of the nonzero decomposable six-forms, even only projectively and even allowing the global sign ±, forces equality of support planes:


G(A⊕ℓ)=A^⊥⊕R_prodℓ


for every A∈Gr(5,I₁₀) and every line ℓ⊂N₂.


E. First intersection: the normal two-plane is forced


Fix one normal line ℓ and intersect the preceding relation over all internal five-planes A. Since


⋂_{A∈Gr(5,I₁₀)} A = {0}


and likewise


⋂_{A∈Gr(5,I₁₀)} A^⊥ = {0},


we have


⋂_A(A⊕ℓ)=ℓ,


⋂_A(A^⊥⊕R_prodℓ)=R_prodℓ.


Invertibility of G commutes with intersections, hence


Gℓ=R_prodℓ


for every normal line ℓ⊂N₂.


So any hypothetical G is already forced to carry the normal projective line exactly according to R_prod.


F. Second intersection: every internal five-plane would have to go to its complement


Now fix A and choose two distinct normal lines ℓ₁,ℓ₂⊂N₂. Then


(A⊕ℓ₁)∩(A⊕ℓ₂)=A.


Their required images satisfy


(A^⊥⊕R_prodℓ₁)∩(A^⊥⊕R_prodℓ₂)=A^⊥,


because R_prod is invertible and therefore sends distinct lines to distinct lines. Applying G to the first intersection gives


G(A)=A^⊥


for every A∈Gr(5,I₁₀).


Thus the desired pair action would require one single linear transformation to realize the Grassmannian complement map


A↦A^⊥


on every internal five-plane.


G. Grassmannian complement is not induced by a linear vector transformation


Take any nonzero v∈I₁₀. For every five-plane A containing v,


Gv∈G(A)=A^⊥.


Therefore


Gv∈⋂_{A∋v}A^⊥.


But this intersection is {0}. Indeed, for every nonzero candidate w∈I₁₀ one can choose a five-plane A containing v and also a vector having nonzero inner product with w, so w∉A^⊥. In particular the positive-definite internal metric removes any null exception.


Hence


Gv=0.


This contradicts invertibility of G.


Theorem 16.I.101.T2 — Grassmannian-duality no-go.


There exists no invertible linear transformation G of the sixteen-dimensional vector space V whose six-form action satisfies


Λ⁶G|_{Λ⁵I₁₀⊗N₂}
=
±(*₁₀⊗R_prod).


The obstruction is the internal Hodge complement itself: on decomposable five-forms it implements the Grassmannian duality A↦A^⊥, and that duality is not the exterior-power action of any single invertible vector transformation.


Corollary 16.I.101.C1 — Universal full-E8 primitive-lift no-go for Q_Y.


No Lie-algebra automorphism of e8(-24) preserving the audited odd chiral 128 can restrict to a primitive q′ whose pair action on the complete certified same-grade five-form normal corridor is ±Q_Y.


This closes the modified-full-E8-lift loophole left open in §16.I.100. The failure is universal within the current parent decomposition; it is not a defect of the particular quarter-dressed q of §16.I.96.


H. What this does and does not kill


The result kills Path B of §16.I.100:


modified full-E8-compatible q′ with the same complete Q_Y pair quotient — NO-GO.


It does not kill the exact reduced primitive q already earned in §16.I.96. That q remains a lawful real involution on the complete odd 128, has


q²=I,


and descends on the certified physical pair corridor to


Λ²q=±Q_Y.


What §16.I.100 and the present section jointly prove is that this product-duality symmetry cannot simultaneously be promoted to an automorphism of the full pre-soldered E8 parent while retaining the complete five-form Hodge quotient.


The earlier compensated exchange


g_X=r_Ir_a∈Spin(12,4)


also survives exactly as before. It is full-E8 compatible but has the wrong internal pair action because its orientation-reversing internal compensator exchanges 126 and bar126 rather than supplying *₁₀. Section 16.I.101 shows that this mismatch is not repairable by choosing a more clever full-E8-compatible Spin representative.


The independent §16.I.94 bracket-kernel theorem also survives unchanged:


inherited tree-level N₂₀ exchange vanishes on the physical 8008 current.


Nothing in the present no-go proves that the independent Hermitian 30380 contact coefficient vanishes, and nothing here derives q covariance of the full reduced action or its measure beyond the already-stated conditional audits.


I. Updated live frontier


EXACT / CLOSED

§16.I.102 — Kinetic-Mismatch Spurion, Canonical-Normalization Obstruction, and the Unique q-Fixed Surface Z_+=Z_-


Status. EXACT FIXED-SURFACE AND CANONICAL-NORMALIZATION THEOREM FOR THE DECLARED TWO-GRADE WEYL KINETIC ANSATZ; CONDITIONAL RG-STABILITY COROLLARY IF THE FULL REDUCED ACTION, MEASURE, AND RENORMALIZATION PRESERVE q; DOES NOT DYNAMICALLY DERIVE THE FIXED SURFACE. THE PURE HERMITIAN 30380 CONTACT NO-GO WAS ALREADY CLOSED IN §16.I.96 AND IS NOT REOPENED HERE.


Section 16.I.101 closed the possibility that the complete heavy product-duality pair action Q_Y could arise from any full-E8-compatible primitive lift preserving the audited odd 128. The only surviving product-duality route is therefore the exact reduced/post-soldering primitive involution q of §16.I.96. The first unresolved condition on that route is the equality of the two grade kinetic coefficients.


A. Declared two-grade kinetic ansatz


Let K_+ and K_- denote the two already-declared soldered Weyl kinetic functionals on the D-grade ±1 odd bundles, including each grade's required conjugate real-form completion. Write


S_kin = Z_+ K_+ + Z_- K_-,


with


Z_+>0,
Z_->0.


The exact reduced primitive involution q exchanges the two D grades and satisfies


q Z q^{-1}=-Z.


At principal-symbol level, with the convention already fixed in §16.I.96,


q: K_+ ↔ K_-.


Thus q covariance of the two-grade kinetic term requires


Z_+ ↔ Z_-.


Introduce the geometric mean and logarithmic mismatch


bar Z := sqrt(Z_+ Z_-),


delta_Z := (1/2) log(Z_+/Z_-).


Equivalently, on the odd 128,


𝒵 = Z_+ Π_+ + Z_- Π_-
   = bar Z exp(delta_Z Z).


Under q,


bar Z → bar Z,


delta_Z → -delta_Z.


Therefore delta_Z is an exact q-odd kinetic spurion.


Theorem 16.I.102.T1 — Unique positive kinetic q-fixed surface.


For the declared two-grade kinetic ansatz with Z_±>0,


q S_kin = S_kin


if and only if


Z_+=Z_-.


Equivalently,


delta_Z=0.


This is the unique positive fixed surface of the q action on the two kinetic coefficients.


This theorem is a symmetry/fixed-surface statement. It does not derive a microscopic reason that the theory must lie on that surface.


B. Canonical normalization does not remove the mismatch


One might attempt to regard unequal Z_± as a field-normalization convention. The exact primitive q shows that this is not sufficient.


Factor out the common q-even normalization bar Z. With the convention


widehat Ψ = D_Z Ψ,


D_Z := exp[(delta_Z/2) Z],


the grade-dependent kinetic matrix is canonically normalized up to the common overall factor bar Z.


The primitive exchange acting on the canonically normalized field is


q_can := D_Z q D_Z^{-1}.


Because


q Z q^{-1}=-Z,


we have


q D_Z^{-1}=D_Z q,


and therefore


q_can = exp(delta_Z Z) q.


The involution property survives:


q_can^2=I.


Thus unequal grade normalization can indeed be shifted out of the kinetic coefficients, but it reappears as a non-orthogonal grade-dependent dressing of the primitive exchange.


C. Pair descent after canonical normalization


On a two-fermion tensor product, the grade generator is additive:


Z^(2)=Z⊗I+I⊗Z.


On the certified same-grade normal pair corridor,


Z^(2)=2 Z_N,


where


Z_N n_±=±n_±.


The exact §16.I.96 primitive q descends there as


Λ²q|_normal = ±Q_Y,


Q_Y=*₁₀⊗R_prod.


Consequently the canonically normalized primitive exchange descends as


Λ²q_can|_normal
 = ± exp(2 delta_Z Z_N) Q_Y.


The extra factor is real and grade dependent. It rescales the two normal lines oppositely:


n_+ : exp(+2 delta_Z),


n_- : exp(-2 delta_Z).


It is not part of the already-earned orthogonal product-duality involution Q_Y.


Theorem 16.I.102.T2 — Canonical-normalization obstruction.


For positive Z_±,


Λ²q_can|_normal = ±Q_Y


if and only if


delta_Z=0,


hence if and only if


Z_+=Z_-.


Proof. Since Z_N has eigenvalues ±1, exp(2 delta_Z Z_N)=I requires both exp(2 delta_Z)=1 and exp(-2 delta_Z)=1. For real delta_Z this occurs only at delta_Z=0. The converse is immediate. ∎


Corollary 16.I.102.C1 — The ratio Z_+/Z_- is physical relative to the earned q/Q_Y structure.


Unequal Z_± cannot be dismissed merely by independent canonical rescalings if one simultaneously insists that the primitive exchange retain the exact unit-normalized pair quotient ±Q_Y. The mismatch is transferred from the kinetic matrix into the symmetry operator and its pair action.


D. Conditional radiative-stability statement


There is a useful but strictly conditional quantum consequence. Suppose all of the following hold in the reduced/post-soldering theory:


1. the full reduced classical action is exactly q invariant at delta_Z=0;
2. the functional measure has no q anomaly;
3. gauge fixing and regulator preserve q, or symmetry-restoring counterterms are available;
4. every other q-odd coupling or background spurion is also set to its q-fixed value.


Then the renormalized effective action obeys the same q covariance. Since q sends


delta_Z→-delta_Z,


the beta function of the mismatch is odd on the q-symmetric locus:


beta_delta(-delta_Z)=-beta_delta(delta_Z),


with the other q-odd spurions fixed to zero. Hence


beta_delta(0)=0.


Corollary 16.I.102.C2 — Conditional RG stability.


If q is an exact non-anomalous symmetry of the complete reduced quantum theory, then the surface Z_+=Z_- is radiatively stable: renormalization cannot generate a q-odd kinetic mismatch from a q-symmetric starting point.


This is not yet an earned quantum theorem of the present model because the complete reduced action, measure, and global anomaly audit remain open.


E. The independent 30380 contact does not rescue or spoil this fixed-surface logic by itself


Section 16.I.96 already proved the exact Hermitian-contact no-go. On the physical pair corridor Q_Y is orthogonal/unitary, so the pure Hermitian 30380 norm is q-even. Therefore q cannot force


c_contact=0.


Conversely, a nonzero coefficient multiplying that q-even norm does not by itself generate the q-odd spurion delta_Z. Thus the kinetic fixed-surface statement and the contact-protection failure are logically distinct:


• Z_+=Z_- is required by q symmetry and is conditionally radiatively stable if q is exact and non-anomalous;


• c_contact=0 is not required by q symmetry and remains an independent microscopic/Wilsonian problem.


F. Updated live frontier


EXACT / CLOSED


• delta_Z=(1/2)log(Z_+/Z_-) is q odd.


• Z_+=Z_- is the unique positive q-fixed kinetic surface.


• Canonical field normalization moves any mismatch into q_can=exp(delta_Z Z)q.


• Its pair descent is ±exp(2 delta_Z Z_N)Q_Y.


• The exact unit-normalized Q_Y quotient is preserved under canonical normalization if and only if Z_+=Z_-.


• The pure Hermitian 30380 contact remains q even; q does not protect c_contact=0.


CONDITIONAL


• If the complete reduced action, measure, regulator, and vacuum are q invariant and q non-anomalous, then beta_delta(0)=0 and the kinetic equality surface is radiatively stable.


OPEN


• what microscopic principle, if any, places the theory on Z_+=Z_- in the first place;


• complete q covariance of the actually declared reduced selector/background/gauge couplings;


• the reduced-theory fermion measure and discrete/global anomaly/eta invariant;


• finite Wilsonian generation of the symmetry-allowed independent 30380 Hermitian contact;


• infrared mirror-grade removal;


• final healthy fermionic and spin-2 spectrum.


Book-16 live gate.


KINETIC q FIXED SURFACE: Z_+=Z_- EXACTLY AND UNIQUELY.


DYNAMICAL SELECTION OF THAT SURFACE: OPEN.


q PROTECTION OF c_contact=0: NO-GO, ALREADY CLOSED IN §16.I.96.


NEXT MICROSCOPIC GATE: AUDIT THE COMPLETE DECLARED REDUCED SELECTOR/BACKGROUND/GAUGE COUPLINGS AND THEN THE q MEASURE/ANOMALY CONDITION.




§16.I.103 — Reduced Kinetic-Algebra Normalizer, Parity-Related Background Covariance, and the Selector-Extension Gate


Status. EXACT REDUCED KINETIC-ALGEBRA NORMALIZER THEOREM FOR THE §16.I.96 PRIMITIVE q; EXACT SPIN(10) CHARGE PRESERVATION AND PARITY-RELATED LORENTZ-BACKGROUND COVARIANCE; EXACT NO-INHERITANCE RESULT FOR A FULL-PARENT SELECTOR EXTENSION FROM §§16.I.100–101; FULL FIXED-VACUUM/SELECTOR q COVARIANCE REMAINS OPEN.


Section 16.I.102 isolated the kinetic mismatch delta_Z and proved that Z_+=Z_- is the unique positive q-fixed kinetic surface. The next question is whether the already-declared reduced covariant derivative itself is compatible with q. The answer splits cleanly between the internal gauge algebra, the physical Lorentz algebra, and the selected bosonic background.


A. Exact reduced q data


Recall the exact §16.I.96 construction. Choose one physical Lorentz reflection r_ell and the conformal-normal reflection r_a, and set


g_ell=r_ell r_a,


P_ell:=rho(g_ell).


Let J_I be the real internal complex structure defined by the oriented ten-dimensional internal Clifford volume element. Book 16 established


J_I^2=-I,


[J_I,Z]=0,


[J_I,P_ell]=0,


and J_I is Spin(10)-equivariant. Define


R_D:=exp[(pi/4) Z J_I],


q:=R_D P_ell.


Then


qZq^{-1}=-Z,


q Pi_±=Pi_∓ q,


q^2=I,


and on the certified same-grade five-form normal pair


Lambda^2 q|_normal=±Q_Y.


Nothing below changes the §§16.I.100–101 result that this q is not the restriction of a full pre-soldered E8 automorphism.


B. Exact internal Spin(10) covariance


Let T in so(10), with odd-spinor action rho_I(T). The factors entering q are all compatible with the internal action:


1. Z is the conformal grading generator and commutes with the internal Spin(10) algebra;


2. J_I is explicitly Spin(10)-equivariant;


3. P_ell is built from a physical Lorentz reflection and a conformal-normal reflection, with no internal reflection factor.


Therefore


[q,rho_I(T)]=0,


or equivalently


q rho_I(T) q^{-1}=rho_I(T).


Theorem 16.I.103.T1 — Internal gauge-charge preservation.


The exact reduced primitive q preserves the internal Spin(10) representation carried by the odd 128. It does not exchange 16 with bar16. At representation level the minimal internal gauge connection A_10 may therefore be assigned q-even transformation law


A_10 -> A_10,


with the ordinary spacetime pullback understood if q is implemented as a parity transformation of the base.


This is an exact representation-level covariance statement. It is not by itself a proof that every possible reduced interaction involving A_10 is q invariant.


C. Exact physical Lorentz normalizer


Let M in so(3,1)_L. The dressing R_D commutes with the physical Lorentz action: Z is conformal-normal and J_I is internal. The conformal-normal reflection r_a likewise commutes with the physical Lorentz algebra. The only nontrivial Lorentz action in P_ell is the chosen physical reflection r_ell. Hence


q rho_L(M) q^{-1}
=
rho_L(Ad_{r_ell} M).


Because a Lorentz reflection normalizes so(3,1),


Ad_{r_ell} so(3,1)=so(3,1).


Theorem 16.I.103.T2 — Reduced kinetic-algebra normalizer.


Let


h_kin:=so(3,1)_L ⊕ so(10)_I.


Then the exact reduced primitive q normalizes h_kin:


q rho(h_kin) q^{-1}=rho(h_kin).


Its action is trivial on the internal Spin(10) algebra and is the reflection automorphism Ad_{r_ell} on the physical Lorentz algebra.


This is fully compatible with §§16.I.100–101. Those sections prove failure to normalize the complete pre-soldered Spin(12,4)/E8 parent structure; the present theorem concerns only the already-reduced post-soldering kinetic algebra.


D. Minimal covariant derivative


The declared soldered Weyl kinetic operator uses


nabla_{omega,A}
=
d + rho_L(omega) + rho_I(A_10).


At the representation/background level, Theorems 16.I.103.T1–T2 give


q nabla_{omega,A} q^{-1}
=
nabla_{omega^(ell),A},


where


omega^(ell):=Ad_{r_ell} omega,


again supplemented by the usual pullback of differential forms if the Lorentz reflection is realized by a spacetime parity map.


Corollary 16.I.103.C1 — Exact parity-related background covariance.


The minimal reduced fermion covariant derivative is exactly mapped by q to the same operator on the parity-related Lorentz background, with the same internal Spin(10) gauge representation and gauge connection.


Thus there is no internal gauge-charge obstruction to the reduced q symmetry at the level of the declared minimal Weyl derivative.


E. Why this is not yet fixed-background kinetic-action invariance


The primitive q contains the chosen physical Lorentz reflection r_ell. Consequently it reverses physical Lorentz orientation. Operator covariance between


(e_F,omega,A_10)


and its reflected background


(e_F^(ell),omega^(ell),A_10)


does not by itself imply pointwise invariance of a generic fixed oriented coframe/selector vacuum.


For the full declared two-grade kinetic action to be q invariant rather than merely covariant between parity-related backgrounds, at least the following must hold:


1. Z_+=Z_- as proved necessary in §16.I.102;


2. the coframe and spin connection must be mapped by the physical reflection to the same physical configuration or to a gauge/diffeomorphism-equivalent configuration;


3. the orientation and volume-form convention in the four-form Weyl action must be transformed consistently;


4. any additional selector-dependent background tensors entering the reduced kinetic operator must have a declared q transformation law and satisfy the same covariance audit.


These are bosonic-background conditions. The exact odd-bundle construction of q alone does not prove them.


F. Selector-extension gate


The exact odd action gives


qZq^{-1}=-Z,


so the D grading line and the grade projectors transform correctly:


q Pi_±=Pi_∓ q.


However, §§16.I.100–101 have now closed the stronger possibility that this transformation is inherited from a full parent E8 automorphism carrying the complete ±Q_Y pair quotient. Therefore one may not promote the odd-bundle q to a transformation of the full selector, coframe, connection, and transverse bosonic parent fields merely by appealing to E8 covariance.


The correct disposition is:


FULL PARENT-INHERITED q SELECTOR EXTENSION WITH COMPLETE Q_Y PAIR QUOTIENT: CLOSED NEGATIVELY.


REDUCED / POST-SOLDERING DISCONNECTED BOSONIC-SELECTOR EXTENSION: REPRESENTATIONALLY AVAILABLE IN PRINCIPLE BUT NOT YET DECLARED OR AUDITED.


SELECTED-VACUUM q INVARIANCE OR PARITY-EQUIVALENCE: OPEN.


This is not a no-go against every reduced bosonic extension. It is a no-inheritance theorem: any surviving extension must be separately specified and tested in the reduced theory.


G. No undeclared Yukawa audit


The live Book-16 action still contains no fully declared independent Yukawa vertex whose q parity can be computed. None is introduced here. Any future Yukawa or condensate-matter coupling must be audited only after its tensor map and coefficient are explicitly declared.


H. Updated live frontier


EXACT / CLOSED


• q commutes with the internal Spin(10) algebra and preserves the internal 16 charge rather than exchanging 16 with bar16.


• the minimal internal gauge connection A_10 is q even at representation level.


• q normalizes the physical Lorentz algebra by the chosen reflection automorphism Ad_{r_ell}.


• q therefore normalizes the complete reduced minimal kinetic algebra so(3,1)_L⊕so(10)_I.


• the declared minimal covariant derivative is exactly covariant between parity-related Lorentz backgrounds.


• §§16.I.100–101 forbid treating the required complete q/Q_Y symmetry as an inherited full-E8 parent automorphism.


CONDITIONAL

Book-16 live gate.

INTERNAL SPIN(10) GAUGE-CHARGE OBSTRUCTION: NONE AT REPRESENTATION LEVEL.


FULL PARENT-INHERITED SELECTOR q EXTENSION: CLOSED NEGATIVELY.

§16.I.104 — Quarter-Axial Index Divisibility, Oriented-Measure Closure, and the Residual Z2 Pin–Spin(10) Global Gate


Status. EXACT ORIENTED-SPIN INDEX-DIVISIBILITY AND FUJIKAWA-CLOSURE THEOREM FOR THE §16.I.96 QUARTER-ROTATION DRESSING; ORDINARY PERTURBATIVE SPIN(10) LOCAL ANOMALY OBSTRUCTION ABSENT; THE GENUINELY ORIENTATION-REVERSING PIN/ETA-INVARIANT q ANOMALY REMAINS OPEN. THIS SECTION STRENGTHENS THE LOCAL-MEASURE STATEMENT ALREADY PRESENT IN §16.I.96 AND DOES NOT CLAIM A FULL QUANTUM q SYMMETRY.


Section 16.I.103 proved that the exact reduced primitive q normalizes the post-soldering kinetic algebra so(3,1)_L⊕so(10)_I and preserves the internal Spin(10) charge. The next question is whether the fermion measure itself obstructs q. The answer again splits sharply between oriented four-dimensional backgrounds and genuinely orientation-reversing backgrounds.


A. Relation to the earlier §16.I.96 measure statement


Section 16.I.96 already established two exact finite-dimensional facts on the complete real odd 128:


• det q=+1;


• the finite-mode Grassmann Jacobian is therefore trivial.


It also stated that, if the regulator preserves the q pairing of the two grade corridors, the ordinary local/Fujikawa perturbative measure phase cancels between the vectorlike partners.


That result deliberately did not evaluate an APS eta invariant, a five-dimensional bordism class, or an anomaly on an unorientable background.


The present section strengthens the oriented-background statement by computing the actual quarter-rotation index phase carried by


R_D=exp[(pi/4)ZJ_I].


B. Dirac form of the quarter dressing


On the paired complex Spin(10)-16 Dirac corridor, the D grading distinguishes the two four-dimensional Weyl chiralities while the internal complex structure J_I acts as multiplication by ±i on the complex half-spin representation and its conjugate.


Fix the chirality convention so that on the physical 16 corridor


ZJ_I = i gamma_5.


Changing the convention gamma_5→-gamma_5 only complex-conjugates the intermediate phase and does not affect the final divisibility result.


Therefore


R_D
=
exp[(pi/4)ZJ_I]
=
exp[i(pi/4)gamma_5],


and the reduced primitive may be written schematically on this paired Dirac corridor as


q
=
exp[i(pi/4)gamma_5] P_ell,


where P_ell is the physical parity/reflection exchange already audited in §16.I.103.


Thus the local measure question associated with the nontrivial dressing is exactly the discrete axial Jacobian of a pi/4 chiral rotation.


C. Twisted Spin(10)-16 index on a closed oriented spin four-manifold


Let X be a closed oriented spin four-manifold and let V→X be the real rank-10 vector bundle associated with a Spin(10) principal bundle. Let S^+(V) denote the complex positive half-spin bundle of V, of complex rank 16.


By the splitting principle, let ±x_i, i=1,...,5, be the formal roots of V_C. The half-spin Chern character is


ch(S^+(V))
=
(1/2)
[
product_i(e^{x_i/2}+e^{-x_i/2})
+
product_i(e^{x_i/2}-e^{-x_i/2})
].


The second product begins in cohomological degree 10 and therefore does not contribute in four dimensions. Expanding the first product through degree four gives


ch(S^+(V))
=
16 + 2 p_1(V) + higher degree.


The A-hat class is


Ahat(TX)
=
1 - p_1(TX)/24 + higher degree.


Hence the Atiyah–Singer index of the four-dimensional Dirac operator twisted by the Spin(10) 16 is


Ind(D_16)
=
∫_X [Ahat(TX) ch(S^+(V))]_4
=
∫_X
[
2p_1(V) - (2/3)p_1(TX)
].


Because V is a Spin bundle,


p_1(V)=2 lambda(V),


with lambda(V) an integral degree-four class. Therefore


2∫_X p_1(V)
=
4∫_X lambda(V)
∈4Z.


For the tangent contribution, the Hirzebruch signature theorem gives


∫_X p_1(TX)=3 sigma(X).


Since X is spin, Rokhlin's theorem gives


sigma(X)∈16Z.


Consequently


-(2/3)∫_X p_1(TX)
=
-2 sigma(X)
∈32Z.


Theorem 16.I.104.T1 — Spin(10)-16 quarter-index divisibility.


For every closed oriented spin four-manifold X and every Spin(10) bundle V→X,


Ind(D_16)∈4Z.


This is stronger than the divisibility needed below. It is a topological statement about the actual twisted Spin(10)-16 Dirac operator and does not rely on perturbation theory or a particular instanton embedding.


D. Exact Fujikawa phase of the pi/4 rotation


For a finite axial rotation


Psi→exp(i alpha gamma_5)Psi,


the regulated fermion measure acquires the standard index phase


J(alpha)
=
exp[-2 i alpha Ind(D_16)],


up to the conventional overall sign in the definition of the Euclidean chiral Jacobian.


For the actual R_D dressing,


alpha=pi/4,


so


J(R_D)
=
exp[-i pi Ind(D_16)/2].


By Theorem 16.I.104.T1, write


Ind(D_16)=4k,


k∈Z.


Then


J(R_D)
=
exp[-2pi i k]
=
1.


Theorem 16.I.104.T2 — Exact oriented quarter-rotation measure closure.


On every closed oriented spin four-manifold with arbitrary Spin(10) background bundle,


J(R_D)=1.


Therefore the pi/4 internal-grade dressing required by the exact reduced primitive q has no residual local or topological Fujikawa phase on oriented spin four-manifolds.


Corollary 16.I.104.C1 — Strengthening of §16.I.96.


The oriented-background q-measure cancellation does not merely follow from finite-mode det q=+1 or from pairing a regulator by hand. The actual quarter-rotation factor itself has identically trivial index phase because the Spin(10)-16 twisted Dirac index is divisible by four.


E. Ordinary perturbative local anomaly ledger


The remaining standard four-dimensional local anomaly tests also give no obstruction for the declared Spin(10)-16 corridor.


1. Pure Spin(10) perturbative gauge anomaly.


A four-dimensional chiral gauge anomaly requires a nonzero symmetric cubic invariant d^{ABC}=tr_R(T^{(A}T^BT^{C)}). The Lie algebra so(10) has no primitive cubic Casimir. Therefore the perturbative local Spin(10)^3 anomaly vanishes for the 16.


2. Mixed Spin(10)-gravity perturbative anomaly.


The coefficient is proportional to tr_R(T^A), which vanishes for every generator of the semisimple algebra so(10). Hence the mixed gauge-gravity triangle vanishes.


3. Pure perturbative gravitational anomaly.


There is no local pure gravitational anomaly for ordinary chiral fermions in four spacetime dimensions.


Theorem 16.I.104.T3 — Oriented local anomaly closure for the declared minimal fermion corridor.


For the actually declared paired Spin(10)-16 Weyl content, no ordinary four-dimensional perturbative Spin(10) gauge anomaly, mixed Spin(10)-gravitational anomaly, or pure local gravitational anomaly obstructs the reduced q construction. Together with Theorem 16.I.104.T2, the oriented local fermion-measure gate is closed.


This theorem concerns the declared minimal fermion corridor. It does not audit hypothetical undeclared Yukawa sectors, additional chiral matter, or a different ultraviolet completion.


F. Why the full q anomaly is still not closed


The primitive q is not merely the internal quarter rotation R_D. It also contains the physical orientation-reversing reflection P_ell. Once one asks whether q is a true quantum symmetry on arbitrary backgrounds, the appropriate Euclidean structure is no longer described solely by an oriented Spin manifold and an ordinary axial Jacobian.


One must specify how the physical reflection lifts after Wick rotation. Depending on that lift and on how fermion parity is identified with the Spin(10) center and the quarter-rotation dressing, the relevant global symmetry structure is expected to be of Pin-like Spin(10) type, schematically


Pin^± ×_{Z_2^F} Spin(10),


or a closely related twisted extension.


The Lorentzian relation


q^2=I


on the reduced odd bundle is not sufficient by itself to choose Pin^+ versus Pin^- in Euclidean signature. Reflection square, Wick-rotation convention, the internal central element, and the R_D dressing all matter.


Known cobordism classifications of orientation-reversing fermionic symmetries with Spin(10) gauge structure leave a possible Z_2 five-dimensional global anomaly class for the relevant Pin–Spin(10) symmetry types. The present Book-16 data do not determine whether the actual reduced q realizes the trivial or nontrivial class.


The correct remaining invariant is therefore a five-dimensional eta/mod-two index or equivalent bordism invariant evaluated for the explicitly defined Euclidean q symmetry structure.


Theorem 16.I.104.T4 — Residual global-anomaly localization.


After the exact oriented closures above, any remaining fermion-measure obstruction to the reduced q symmetry is genuinely global and orientation reversing. It cannot be detected by the ordinary four-dimensional perturbative anomaly polynomial or by the oriented pi/4 Fujikawa phase.


The remaining gate is localized to the Euclidean Pin-like extension of q and its five-dimensional eta/bordism invariant.


This is a localization theorem, not a cancellation theorem for that final class.

§16.I.105 — q-Twisted Dirac Mass, Pin–Spin(10) Global-Anomaly Trivialization, and the Euclidean-Square Residue


Status. EXACT REDUCED-FERMION GLOBAL-ANOMALY CLOSURE FOR THE TWO-GRADE q-PAIRED DIRAC-16, PROVIDED THE EUCLIDEAN q TRANSITION FUNCTION IS THE CONTINUATION OF THE ALREADY-EARNED REDUCED OPERATOR. THE PIN SIGN REMAINS A GEOMETRIC CONTINUATION CHOICE. THIS SECTION DOES NOT CLAIM THAT THE PHYSICAL THEORY DYNAMICALLY GENERATES A FERMION MASS, REMOVES THE MIRROR GRADE, OR MAKES THE FULL BOSONIC ACTION/VACUUM q-INVARIANT.


Section 16.I.104 closed every oriented four-dimensional measure obstruction that can be computed from the declared minimal Spin(10)-16 corridor. The remaining question was whether the orientation-reversing q symmetry could carry the nontrivial element of the available five-dimensional Pin–Spin(10) anomaly group. The present section answers that reduced-fermion question directly by constructing a globally q-equivariant gauge-singlet gapping mass/regulator.


A. Euclidean Pin square and the quarter dressing


Let the Euclidean continuation of the reduced primitive be


q_E = R_D P_E,


R_D = exp[(pi/4) Z J_I],


where P_E is an orientation-reversing Euclidean lift satisfying the continued reduced relations


P_E Z P_E^{-1} = -Z,


[P_E,J_I]=0.


Then


P_E R_D P_E^{-1}=R_D^{-1}.


Therefore


q_E^2
=
R_D P_E R_D P_E
=
R_D R_D^{-1} P_E^2
=
P_E^2.


Theorem 16.I.105.T1 — Pin-square neutrality of the quarter dressing.


The q quarter rotation does not alter the Euclidean reflection square:


q_E^2=P_E^2.


Thus the distinction between Pin^+ and Pin^- is inherited entirely from the Euclidean reflection lift P_E. The Lorentzian relation q^2=I does not by itself choose that continuation.


Section 16.I.103 also proved


[q, rho_I(Spin(10))]=0.


Hence q does not conjugate Spin(10) by an outer reflection. The relevant Euclidean symmetry candidates are therefore the direct central products


G_E^± = Pin^±(4) ×_{Z_2^F} Spin(10),


not semidirect Pin–Spin(10) extensions in which orientation reversal acts nontrivially on the gauge group.


B. Exact q transformation of the Dirac mass angle


On the paired complex Dirac-16 corridor write


q=A_alpha P,


A_alpha=exp(i alpha gamma_5),


alpha=pi/4,


with


P gamma_5 P^{-1}=-gamma_5.


Consider the gauge-singlet complex Dirac mass bilinear


B_theta = bar(Psi) exp(i theta gamma_5) Psi.


Under the axial factor A_alpha, both Psi and bar(Psi) acquire the same chiral factor in the bilinear, so theta shifts by 2alpha. The subsequent physical reflection reverses gamma_5. Therefore


q:


theta -> -(theta+2alpha).


The fixed-angle condition is


theta = -(theta+2alpha) mod 2pi,


or


theta=-alpha mod pi.


For alpha=pi/4 one obtains the q-even gauge-singlet mass


L_{m,q}
=
m bar(Psi) exp[-i(pi/4)gamma_5] Psi.


Equivalently, in chiral components,


L_{m,q}
=
m[ e^{-i pi/4} bar(psi_L) psi_R + e^{+i pi/4} bar(psi_R) psi_L ].


Theorem 16.I.105.T2 — q-fixed Dirac mass angle.


For the exact reduced primitive q, the two-grade Spin(10)-16 Dirac corridor admits a nonzero Spin(10)-singlet bilinear mass invariant under q. Its phase is fixed by the same quarter angle already required by the primitive lift.


This mass necessarily couples the two D grades. It is therefore an anomaly-test deformation of the complete q-paired corridor, not a proof that one grade can be discarded while q remains exact.


C. Global descent on an orientation-reversing q bundle


The expression exp[-i(pi/4)gamma_5] is convenient on an oriented chart, but gamma_5 is not itself a globally defined scalar on a generic unorientable manifold. The correct global test is transition-function descent.


Take an atlas of locally oriented Euclidean charts. On orientation-preserving overlaps, the mass is an ordinary Spin(4)×Spin(10) scalar. On an orientation-reversing overlap, the fermion transition function is q_E. The reflection exchanges the local left- and right-handed bundles, while the quarter dressing supplies their compensating phases.


The two local terms


e^{-i pi/4} bar(psi_L) psi_R


and


e^{+i pi/4} bar(psi_R) psi_L


are exchanged by the orientation-reversing q transition. Their sum is therefore invariant on the overlap. No globally defined gamma_5 is required: the bilinear is defined intrinsically by q-equivariant patching.


Theorem 16.I.105.T3 — Global q-equivariant mass descent.


For either allowed Euclidean reflection square, the q-fixed two-grade Dirac mass descends to a globally defined Spin(10)-gauge-invariant scalar on the corresponding q-Pin bundle.


The proof uses only the exact grade exchange, P gamma_5 P^{-1}=-gamma_5, and the fixed quarter angle. It does not depend on choosing Pin^+ rather than Pin^-.


D. Global anomaly consequence


A nonzero q-preserving gauge-singlet mass gaps the complete reduced two-grade Dirac-16 without breaking Spin(10) or q. The same globally defined bilinear can be used as a symmetry-preserving heavy regulator mass for the reduced fermion determinant.


Therefore this fermion representation does not realize a nontrivial five-dimensional obstruction to defining the q symmetry. In the possible Z_2 anomaly group for either direct-product Pin–Spin(10) continuation, its coefficient is zero:


nu_q(16_D)=0 in Z_2.


Theorem 16.I.105.T4 — Reduced q-Pin–Spin(10) global-anomaly closure.


The exact q-paired two-grade Dirac-16 corridor is globally anomaly-free with respect to the orientation-reversing reduced q symmetry for either Pin sign compatible with its Euclidean continuation.


This statement concerns the anomaly of the declared reduced fermion representation. A symmetry-preserving massive deformation may still define a nontrivial four-dimensional invertible phase for one regulator sign; that is not an 't Hooft anomaly and does not obstruct defining the theory. The theorem asserts the absence of the five-dimensional symmetry obstruction.


E. Oriented half-angle consistency check


On an oriented spin background only, define


U=exp[-i(alpha/2)gamma_5].


Then


U q U^{-1}=P,


and the q-fixed mass is rotated to an ordinary scalar Dirac mass. The associated finite chiral Jacobian is


J(U)=exp[i alpha Ind(D_16)].


For alpha=pi/4 and the §16.I.104 divisibility Ind(D_16)=4k,


J(U)=(-1)^k.


Using


Ind(D_16)/4 = integral_X lambda(V) - sigma(X)/2,


and sigma(X)/2 in 8Z on a spin four-manifold, this becomes


J(U)=(-1)^{integral_X lambda(V)}.


Since lambda(V) mod 2 = w_4(V) for a Spin bundle, the half-angle field redefinition can differ from the parity convention by the oriented local topological sign


(-1)^{integral_X w_4(V)}.


This is a consistency check, not the global proof: U is not a globally defined chiral field redefinition on a generic unorientable q-Pin background. The actual global argument is the q-equivariant mass descent of subsection C.

§16.I.106 — Maximal Declared Bosonic q-Covariance and the Quarter-Vacuum Gauge-Compensation Obstruction


Status. EXACT CERTIFIED-SECTOR q-COVARIANCE LEDGER AND EXACT FAILURE OF THE CURRENTLY CERTIFIED FULL-E8 COMPENSATOR g_X TO RETURN THE PHYSICAL QUARTER VACUUM TO ITSELF. THE EXISTENCE OF A DIFFERENT LAWFUL GAUGE/STABILIZER ELEMENT THAT COMPLETES THE COMPENSATION REMAINS OPEN. THIS SECTION DOES NOT PROMOTE q TO A FULL MICROSCOPIC VACUUM SYMMETRY.


Section 16.I.105 closed the reduced fermion-measure/anomaly gate. The remaining issue is bosonic action covariance and vacuum invariance. Sections 16.I.100–101 already prove that q is not inherited from a full E8 automorphism on all 248 parent directions. Therefore the only lawful audit is on post-soldering fields and terms for which a reduced q action has actually been declared.


A. Maximal presently declared reduced q-covariant sector


The selected-line gravitational sector is already closed by §16.I.99. Under the orientation-compensated physical reflection,


P_ell e_F^a = s^a{}_b e_F^b,


P_ell R^{ab} = s^a{}_c s^b{}_d R^{cd},


with det s = -1. The Einstein–Cartan, Holst and volume terms are coefficientwise q-covariant once the orientation-form sign is transformed consistently. The rank-one coframe projector


P_cf=(1-J_F)/2


is preserved projectively on the quarter-Y balanced line, so this symmetry does not generate an automatic second graviton.


On the reduced odd sector, q commutes with Spin(10), so the Spin(10) covariant derivative is compatible with q. The kinetic term is q-invariant on the already-identified normalization surface Z_+=Z_-. Sections 16.I.104–105 then close the oriented and Pin–Spin(10) measure obstructions for the complete q-paired Dirac-16.


For the selector itself,


qDq^{-1}=-D.


Consequently a q-invariant reduced selector potential, if one is declared, must satisfy


V(D)=V(-D).


Odd selector invariants are forbidden by exact q invariance, while even contractions may occur. This is a symmetry constraint, not a derivation of the selector potential.


Book 16 presently contains no fully declared independent Yukawa vertex with a named bosonic representation and fixed intertwiner. Therefore there is no unique Yukawa q-audit to perform, and none is invented here.


Theorem 16.I.106.T1 — Maximal declared reduced bosonic covariance.


The currently explicit post-soldering q action certifies q covariance of the selected-line gravity/coframe sector and compatibility with the Spin(10)-covariant reduced fermion sector. It does not certify q covariance of the full parent B/selector/bridge system, because §§16.I.100–101 exclude inheritance from a full E8 automorphism and no independent reduced q action has been declared for every remaining parent-derived bosonic field and coupling.


Thus the correct status is


CERTIFIED REDUCED SUBSECTOR: q-COVARIANT,


FULL REDUCED BOSONIC COMPLETION: OPEN.


B. Vacuum invariance is stronger than action covariance


Let Phi_0 denote the complete selected broken-phase vacuum data. At minimum it contains the nonzero selector D, the rank-one coframe data and the physical two-normal condensate C_0.


A genuine unbroken q symmetry requires either


q Phi_0 = Phi_0,


or, modulo gauge equivalence, the existence of a lawful bosonic gauge/stabilizer element h such that


h q Phi_0 = Phi_0.


The selector sign alone cannot decide this question. A compensator must return the complete vacuum orbit, not merely D.


C. Exact quarter-branch discriminator


Write the two-grade condensate amplitudes as a,b with the physical quarter branch


Re(a bar b)=0,


Im(a bar b) != 0.


Define the nonzero orientation pseudoscalar


P_0 := 2 Im(a bar b) != 0.


The already-earned product-duality action induced by q is Hodge-linear and leaves this pseudoscalar even. Therefore on the selector/pseudoscalar data,


q: (D,P_0) -> (-D,+P_0).


By contrast, the certified full-E8 compensated exchange


g_X=r_I r_a in Spin(12,4)


is the exact grade-reversing/full-mirror operation. It reverses the selector and the mirror-odd orientation pseudoscalar,


g_X: (D,P_0) -> (-D,-P_0),


and on the internal five-form sector it exchanges 126 with bar(126), rather than acting by the Hodge-linear Q_Y operator.


Apply the known E8 compensator after q. One obtains


(g_X q):(D,P_0) -> (D,-P_0).


Because the physical quarter branch has P_0 != 0,


(g_X q) Phi_0 != Phi_0


already on this exact two-component vacuum discriminator.


Theorem 16.I.106.T2 — Known-compensator quarter-vacuum obstruction.


The currently certified full-E8 grade-reversal g_X cannot gauge-compensate q on the physical quarter condensate/selector vacuum. Although g_X restores the selector sign after q, it flips the nonzero quarter-branch pseudoscalar P_0 and therefore lands on the opposite full-mirror orientation branch.


Corollary 16.I.106.C1 — Selector-sign gauge equivalence is insufficient.


The fact that D and -D are related by the exact full-E8 exchange g_X does not establish that the q-transformed broken vacuum is gauge-equivalent to the original vacuum. Complete condensate-orbit data must be preserved.


D. What this does not prove


Theorem 16.I.106.T2 is a no-go for the known compensator g_X, not a classification of the entire real E8(-24) or residual broken-phase stabilizer. It remains logically possible that another lawful element h satisfies


hD h^{-1}=-D


while also preserving the quarter pseudoscalar P_0, the condensate orbit, and the selected coframe projector in exactly the way required for


h q Phi_0 = Phi_0.


No such element is presently earned. No theorem presently excludes all such elements.


Accordingly the vacuum status is


KNOWN COMPENSATOR g_X: FAILS,


GLOBAL STABILIZER/GAUGE IDENTIFICATION: OPEN,


UNBROKEN MICROSCOPIC q WARD SYMMETRY: NOT YET EARNED.


E. Consequences for contact protection and mirror dynamics


The fermion anomaly closure of §§16.I.104–105 survives unchanged. The present obstruction is a bosonic/vacuum issue, not a resurrection of the measure problem.


No q Ward identity may presently be invoked as an unbroken-vacuum selection rule.
